# Calradia Remembers: the rules, as its own tests state them

GENERATED by the Maps fixtures in `CalradiaRemembers.Tests/Maps`. Do not edit: every line is lifted from a
test name and a test file's header, so this cannot drift from the behaviour it describes, and a test fails when
this file no longer matches the suite. See `docs/maps/README.md`.

4189 rules across 437 fixtures. A line marked `(not stated as a rule)` is a
test whose name describes a return value or a branch rather than something anyone would
notice; those are shown rather than hidden, because a gap in a specification should be
visible. There are 13 of them. See CLAUDE.md, "What a test is for".

## Actions

### Actions/ActionConfirmationPolicyTests.cs

_Documents which LLM-proposed actions are gated behind an in-chat Y/n confirmation before they touch the player's gold or commitments, how a typed answer is read, and the exact wording shown. The LLM proposing a spend is not the player agreeing to it: per ActionConfirmationController's own header, this also tames the LLM re-emitting the same charge every turn (the reported repeated-charges bug). Feeds ActionConfirmationController.Preprocess (RequiresConfirmation decides Queued vs ExecuteInline), HandleInput (ParseAnswer routes Confirm/Decline/Recompose), and PromptNext (BuildQuestion is the literal line shown)._

- GIVEN an action the player spends or commits on, WHEN checked, THEN it requires confirmation
- GIVEN give gold the npc gives the player coin, WHEN checked, THEN it does not require confirmation
- GIVEN an action type that does not move coin or commit service, WHEN checked, THEN it does not require confirmation
- GIVEN a null action type, WHEN checked, THEN it does not require confirmation
- GIVEN an empty action type, WHEN checked, THEN it does not require confirmation
- GIVEN an empty or affirmative answer, WHEN parsed, THEN it is a confirm
- GIVEN a negative answer, WHEN parsed, THEN it is a decline
- GIVEN an answer that is neither yes nor no, WHEN parsed, THEN it is a recompose
- GIVEN give gold, WHEN building the question, THEN it asks whether the player accepts the coins
- GIVEN take gold, WHEN building the question, THEN it asks whether the player pays the npc
- GIVEN join party, WHEN building the question, THEN it asks whether the npc takes service
- GIVEN free prisoner, WHEN building the question, THEN it asks whether to set the prisoner free ignoring amount
- GIVEN an unrecognized action type, WHEN building the question, THEN it falls back to a generic confirmation

### Actions/ManualActionAvailabilityTests.cs

_Manual action-injection menu (the button beside the purse and the quill): the player picks from the actions POSSIBLE RIGHT NOW against the conversation NPC, injected through the same bridge an LLM action would use. This is the pure gate deciding what appears: get a fact wrong here and the player either sees an entry the bridge will only refuse (a broken-feeling menu) or never sees one they earned (a feature that looks unbuilt). NPC-JUDGMENT actions (change_relation, sway_opinion, give_gold...) never enter this catalog at all (ManualActionCatalog's own header states that boundary); these tests only cover which of the PLAYER-DRIVEN entries a given snapshot of facts exposes or withholds._

- GIVEN a null facts snapshot, WHEN resolving availability, THEN nothing is offered
- GIVEN an otherwise empty conversation, WHEN resolving availability, THEN take gold is always offered
- GIVEN no items and no influence need, WHEN resolving availability, THEN give item and spend influence are withheld
- GIVEN the player carries a giveable item, WHEN resolving availability, THEN give item is offered
- GIVEN the npcs clan can accept backing, WHEN resolving availability, THEN spend influence is offered
- GIVEN the npc is a prisoner the player holds and eligible to turn, WHEN resolving availability, THEN execute free and recruit prisoner are all offered
- GIVEN a held prisoner not yet eligible to turn, WHEN resolving availability, THEN execute and free are offered but recruit is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is not the players prisoner, WHEN resolving availability, THEN no prisoner action is offered
- GIVEN the npc is not a companion, WHEN resolving availability, THEN recall and reassure are withheld even if the secondary fact is set
- GIVEN a companion away on an errand, WHEN resolving availability, THEN recall companion is offered
- GIVEN a companion riding along and not away, WHEN resolving availability, THEN recall companion is withheld
- GIVEN a discontented companion, WHEN resolving availability, THEN reassure companion is offered
- GIVEN a lord who could begin escorting the player, WHEN resolving availability, THEN follow me is offered and dismiss escort is not
- GIVEN a lord already escorting the player, WHEN resolving availability, THEN dismiss escort is offered and follow me is not
- GIVEN an idle lord who could ride along, WHEN resolving availability, THEN ride with me is offered and part ways is not
- GIVEN a retainer already riding along, WHEN resolving availability, THEN part ways is offered and ride with me is not
- GIVEN a dispatchable assignable and rejoinable companion, WHEN resolving availability, THEN all three party command entries are offered
- GIVEN a player who does not rule a kingdom, WHEN resolving availability, THEN every governance entry is withheld
- GIVEN a ruling player with a vassal and a grantable fief, WHEN resolving availability, THEN grant fief is offered
- GIVEN a ruling player with a revocable vassal fief, WHEN resolving availability, THEN revoke fief is offered
- GIVEN a clan member who could be seated over a vacant fief, WHEN resolving availability, THEN appoint governor is offered
- GIVEN a companion who could be put on a stipend, WHEN resolving availability, THEN grant stipend is offered
- GIVEN an npc whose own party is under strength, WHEN resolving availability, THEN give troops is offered
- GIVEN a faction leader the player can mediate with, WHEN resolving availability, THEN mediate peace is offered
- GIVEN a settlement notable eligible to bequeath their post, WHEN resolving availability, THEN recruit notable is offered
- GIVEN a hireable wanderer with a resolved asking price, WHEN resolving availability, THEN join party is offered
- GIVEN no resolved hiring price, WHEN resolving availability, THEN join party is withheld
- GIVEN a lord who may be called out, WHEN the menu is built, THEN the duel is offered
- GIVEN no venue or no admissibility, WHEN the menu is built, THEN the duel is not offered

### Actions/ManualActionStatusTests.cs

_that the panel can tell the player what he may ask, what is merely out of reach today, and what will never apply to this person, from ONE decision rather than three._

- GIVEN any conversation, WHEN the menu asks what is available, THEN it is exactly what the panel calls open
- GIVEN an action he may take, WHEN the panel is built, THEN it is open and says nothing further
- GIVEN an action blocked by where things stand, WHEN the panel is built, THEN it names what is missing
- GIVEN an action that does not apply to this person, WHEN the panel is built, THEN it is not merely blocked
- GIVEN a prisoner who will not yet turn, WHEN the panel is built, THEN it is blocked rather than never
- GIVEN any facts, WHEN the panel is built, THEN every catalogue entry appears exactly once
- GIVEN the emptiest possible conversation, WHEN the panel is built, THEN every closed entry carries a reason
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN the panel is built, THEN nothing is offered and nothing throws

### Actions/TaughtActionSetTests.cs

_Documents which 1:1 [ACTION] verbs a single ordinary conversation turn is deemed to have TAUGHT, given the turn's eligibility facts. This is the pure core of the v1.34.2 log-only execution audit (Pattern 1): the action bridge runs any parsed [ACTION] without checking it was taught, so the host builds this set from the live EncounterContext and logs (never refuses) an execution that is absent from it. Because it is observability, not enforcement, the set is deliberately GENEROUS: a false "taught" only quiets the audit, while a wrong "not taught" would cry wolf, so every mapped gate here mirrors the SDK PromptBuilder's own teaching condition. Get the base set wrong and the audit spams on every routine turn; get a gate wrong and a legitimate action is logged as a hallucination that a future ENFORCING version might then wrongly refuse._

- GIVEN a bare turn with no eligibility, WHEN the set is built, THEN the base action is present
- GIVEN a taught action in different casing, WHEN membership is checked, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN the player is not captive, WHEN the set is built, THEN give item is taught
- GIVEN the player is captive, WHEN the set is built, THEN give item is not taught
- GIVEN the npc can be appointed governor, WHEN the set is built, THEN appoint governor is taught
- GIVEN the npc cannot be appointed governor, WHEN the set is built, THEN appoint governor is not taught
- GIVEN the npc can be dispatched on mission, WHEN the set is built, THEN both dispatch mission and gather news are taught
- GIVEN a recruitable companion, WHEN the set is built, THEN join party is taught
- GIVEN the court verb bundle is offered, WHEN the set is built, THEN the family is taught
- GIVEN the court verb bundle is not offered, WHEN the set is built, THEN the family is not taught
- GIVEN the player holds this npc captive, WHEN the set is built, THEN the prisoner bargain verbs are taught
- GIVEN a bare turn, WHEN the set is built, THEN a clearly untaught action is absent
- GIVEN a base or flow type, WHEN asked if never audited, THEN it is true
- GIVEN a world changing verb, WHEN asked if never audited, THEN it is false

## Appreciations

### Appreciations/AppreciationBackfillRulesTests.cs

_AppreciationBackfillRules decides which persisted markers in an EXISTING save (before this pillar existed) can be safely read back as a felt kindness, feeding AppreciationMigration so old saves gain Kindnesses without a new game. Conservative by construction, mirroring GrudgeBackfillRules: recognizes ONLY the two exact markers documented in the production file's header (a Completed InformalQuest, a persisted Intimacy NotableEvent) and returns null for anything else, per the project's hard save- migration rule (never guess). Too eager here fabricates a kindness the player never earned; too strict silently drops a real one. These tests pin exactly which markers convert and which are skipped._

- GIVEN a completed quest, WHEN mapping, THEN a quest done for them candidate anchors on the satisfied day
- GIVEN a completed quest with no satisfied day, WHEN mapping, THEN it anchors on the issued day
- GIVEN a non intimacy event, WHEN mapping, THEN nothing is produced
- GIVEN a profile event with no holder id, WHEN mapping, THEN it is conservatively skipped
- GIVEN a quest outcome with no giver id, WHEN mapping, THEN it is conservatively skipped
- GIVEN a quest status other than completed, WHEN mapping, THEN nothing is produced
- GIVEN a short summary, WHEN mapping, THEN it is carried verbatim
- GIVEN a summary longer than the cap, WHEN mapping, THEN the cause is truncated
- GIVEN an intimacy ordinal event, WHEN mapping, THEN a kindness in scene candidate is produced

### Appreciations/AppreciationMigrationTests.cs

_SAVE-MIGRATION HARD REQUIREMENT (ROADMAP 2026-07-04), the positive mirror of GrudgeMigration: AppreciationMigration.SynthesizeAppreciations is the one-shot backfill run on every session launch, translating markers already sitting in an OLD save (gift/gold received, ransomed/freed, quest done for them, kindness in scene, divorce gratitude) into first-class Appreciation records. Additive- only, conservative (unknown markers skipped, never guessed), idempotent (dedup by a RECOMPUTED holder+source+day key, never the stored Id), and versioned (a no-op once the ledger is stamped at or beyond MigrationVersion). Get this wrong and a player loading an old save loses their kindness history, or sees it replanted, and double-counted, on every relaunch._

- GIVEN a candidate below the formation floor, WHEN migrating, THEN it is skipped by the same anti noise guardrail
- GIVEN a candidate marked unattributable, WHEN migrating, THEN it is skipped however large
- GIVEN a ledger already stamped at the current version, WHEN migrating again, THEN it is a pure no op
- GIVEN a ledger seeded from a prior pass, WHEN migrating the same candidates again, THEN no new appreciations are produced
- GIVEN a ledger stamped beyond the current version, WHEN migrating, THEN it is still a no op
- GIVEN a migration pass, WHEN it runs, THEN it never mutates the existing appreciations collection it was handed
- GIVEN a save carrying every known marker, WHEN migrating for the first time, THEN an appreciation is synthesized for each
- GIVEN a source felt behind the scenes, WHEN migrating, THEN it defaults unvoiced
- GIVEN a synthesized appreciation, WHEN inspecting it, THEN it carries the original cause and day
- GIVEN an appreciation already covering the same event but carrying a NON canonical id, WHEN migrating, THEN it is still recognized as a duplicate
- GIVEN an empty candidate list, WHEN migrating, THEN nothing is produced
- GIVEN an unrecognized marker, WHEN migrating, THEN it is skipped not guessed at
- GIVEN no candidates at all, WHEN migrating, THEN the result is empty but not a no op
- GIVEN one candidate already present in the ledger, WHEN migrating a mixed batch, THEN only the new one is added
- GIVEN sources the player visibly performed themselves, WHEN migrating, THEN they are known to the player at formation
- GIVEN the same candidates and no ledger, WHEN synthesizing twice independently, THEN the two results are deeply identical

### Appreciations/AppreciationNarratorTests.cs

_AppreciationNarrator turns an Appreciation's raw numbers (Magnitude, decayed EffectiveMagnitude) into the two pieces of prose the mod ships: the encyclopedia's "Kindnesses" sentence and the one-liner injected straight into the NPC's LLM prompt. The pillar is the positive mirror of Grudges and shares its hard surfacing rule, narrative text, no numbers, ever, but carries its OWN twist: unlike a grudge there is no concealment register, an unvoiced appreciation is a quiet warmth, never hidden coldness. A stray digit here breaks the numberless-narrative rule; a wrong knownness branch either blurts out a kindness the NPC has not earned the right to name, or mutes warmth that should be voiced. These tests pin the exact wording and gating so a tweak cannot silently reintroduce either failure._

- GIVEN a cause that ends in a period, WHEN building the display line, THEN the sentence ends in a single period
- GIVEN a fully faded appreciation, WHEN building the display line, THEN nothing is surfaced
- GIVEN a fully faded appreciation, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN nothing is surfaced
- GIVEN a high magnitude, WHEN naming the band, THEN it reads as a deep and lasting gratitude
- GIVEN a known appreciation, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN the npc may name it openly
- GIVEN a low magnitude, WHEN naming the band, THEN it reads as a small kindness remembered
- GIVEN a mid magnitude, WHEN naming the band, THEN it reads as a genuine debt of gratitude
- GIVEN a missing target name, WHEN building the display line, THEN it falls back to them rather than blank
- GIVEN a null appreciation, WHEN building the display line, THEN it returns null not throw
- GIVEN a null appreciation, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN it returns null not throw
- GIVEN a very low magnitude, WHEN naming the band, THEN it reads as a fleeting warmth
- GIVEN an unvoiced appreciation, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN it still warms without being named
- GIVEN every warmth band, WHEN building the display line, THEN no digits ever appear in the generated text
- GIVEN every warmth band, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN no digits ever appear in the generated text
- GIVEN no cause summary, WHEN building the display line, THEN a fallback phrase is used instead of blank text
- GIVEN no cause summary, WHEN building the prompt line, THEN a fallback phrase is used instead of blank text
- GIVEN the appreciation targets a named npc, WHEN building the display line, THEN the npc is named by name
- GIVEN the appreciation targets the player, WHEN building the display line, THEN the player is named you

### Appreciations/AppreciationPolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides three things a chat turn and an NPC's encyclopedia page both depend on: whether a kindness is worth remembering at all (anti-noise floor), how much warmth survives to be voiced today (decay, mirrored to a grudge's own decay so a kindness lingers longer), and which few memories actually surface when several compete (per-NPC cap) or when a fresh betrayal sours them (TarnishedMagnitude). Get the floor wrong and every trivial courtesy clutters the "Kindnesses" encyclopedia section and the LLM prompt with noise; get the cap or the tarnish math wrong and an NPC's felt regard stops tracking what the player actually did, which is the whole premise the CHANGELOG calls "how NPCs feel about you, made coherent."_

- GIVEN a full house but some entries are fully faded, WHEN admitting, THEN only the live ones count toward the cap
- GIVEN a full house, WHEN the newcomer is no stronger than the weakest live appreciation, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a full house, WHEN the newcomer is strictly stronger than the weakest live appreciation, THEN it is admitted and the weakest is named for eviction
- GIVEN a fully faded appreciation, WHEN selecting dominant, THEN it never surfaces
- GIVEN a grudge as strong as the appreciation, WHEN tarnishing, THEN it roughly cancels the warm feeling
- GIVEN a grudge far stronger than the appreciation, WHEN tarnishing, THEN it floors at zero never negative
- GIVEN a null appreciation, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it reads as zero
- GIVEN a null collection, WHEN selecting dominant, THEN it returns empty not null
- GIVEN a null live list, WHEN admitting a newcomer, THEN it is admitted
- GIVEN a softer active grudge, WHEN tarnishing, THEN the warmth is reduced but not wiped out
- GIVEN an already faded appreciation, WHEN tarnishing, THEN it stays at zero regardless of the grudge
- GIVEN an appreciation and a grudge of the same starting magnitude, WHEN the same time elapses, THEN the appreciation has faded less
- GIVEN an unattributable kindness, WHEN deciding formation, THEN it refuses however large
- GIVEN enough elapsed time, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it floors at zero never negative
- GIVEN fewer live appreciations than the cap, WHEN admitting a newcomer, THEN it is always admitted with nothing to evict
- GIVEN high calculating, WHEN deciding formation, THEN the bar is raised and a middling kindness no longer forms
- GIVEN high generosity, WHEN deciding formation, THEN the bar is lowered and a lesser kindness still forms
- GIVEN high generosity, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it decays slower than a plain holder
- GIVEN high mercy, WHEN deciding formation, THEN the bar is also lowered
- GIVEN high mercy, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it also decays slower
- GIVEN magnitude at the floor, WHEN deciding formation, THEN it forms
- GIVEN magnitude below the floor, WHEN deciding formation, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN more live appreciations than the cap, WHEN selecting dominant, THEN only the top ones surface
- GIVEN no active grudge, WHEN tarnishing, THEN the appreciation keeps its full warmth
- GIVEN no time has passed, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it reads as the stored magnitude
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN deciding formation, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the baseline decay rate, WHEN compared to the grudge base rate, THEN it is exactly half
- GIVEN time passing, WHEN reading effective magnitude, THEN it softens

### Appreciations/AppreciationRepetitionTests.cs

_Player-reported (screenshot, 2026-07-22): Corein's encyclopedia showed the SAME kindness three times, "Carries a small kindness remembered toward you, for A tender moment we shared." repeated verbatim._

- GIVEN a record saved before the field existed, WHEN counting, THEN it reads as having happened once
- GIVEN a kindness done twice, WHEN weighing it, THEN it is felt more strongly than once
- GIVEN a kindness repeated again and again, WHEN weighing it, THEN each repeat adds less than the one before
- GIVEN an absurd number of repeats, WHEN weighing it, THEN the bonus stops at the cap
- GIVEN two identical kindnesses one repeated, WHEN read much later, THEN the repeated one still lingers warmer
- GIVEN a kindness done three times, WHEN written, THEN the count is spelled in words not digits
- GIVEN a kindness done once, WHEN written, THEN no repetition clause appears
- GIVEN a kindness repeated beyond counting, WHEN written, THEN it reads as many rather than a tally
- GIVEN a cause stored as its own sentence, WHEN embedded mid sentence, THEN its leading capital is lowered
- GIVEN a cause that opens on a name, WHEN embedded mid sentence, THEN the name keeps its capital
- GIVEN a save holding three identical records, WHEN consolidated, THEN one remains carrying the full tally
- GIVEN twins across several days, WHEN consolidated, THEN the warmth is felt from the most recent one
- GIVEN two different kindnesses from the same source, WHEN consolidated, THEN both survive untouched
- GIVEN an already consolidated ledger, WHEN consolidated again, THEN the tally does not climb

### Appreciations/AppreciationValueScalingTests.cs

_Appreciations pillar: the denar-value-to-magnitude mapping shared by the GiftReceived/GoldReceived live plant sites._

- GIVEN a middling value, WHEN scaling, THEN it divides by the denars per point constant
- GIVEN a tiny value, WHEN scaling, THEN it floors at the minimum magnitude
- GIVEN a zero or negative value, WHEN scaling, THEN it still floors at the minimum magnitude
- GIVEN a huge value, WHEN scaling, THEN it ceilings at the maximum magnitude

## Assemblies

### Assemblies/ArrangeMarriageGroundingCodecTests.cs

_Documents the composite grounding arrange_marriage uses to carry TWO kin names through the single TargetHint slot every other resolution kind reuses. The stake throughout: a codec that fails to round-trip silently loses one of the two kin the council pledged to wed, and CouncilLift.SettleArrangeMarriage would then either resolve the wrong pair or refuse a match the table genuinely struck, exactly the empty-promise class this whole design exists to prevent._

- GIVEN two ordinary kin names, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN both survive unchanged
- GIVEN a null or blank composite, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a composite with no delimiter, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN either half is blank, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN the target kin name itself contains the delimiter, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it survives whole
- GIVEN a null half, WHEN composed, THEN it does not throw and still fails to decompose

### Assemblies/AssemblyArrivalPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when an accepted invitee attends in person versus sends an ambassador: it fits when the journey still fits the deadline. A hero already at the host attends in person even past the deadline; a hero still on the road when the deadline lapses sends a stand-in._

- GIVEN the journey fits the deadline, WHEN resolving, THEN the invitee attends in person
- GIVEN the journey overruns the deadline, WHEN resolving, THEN an ambassador takes the seat
- GIVEN the invitee is already at the host, WHEN resolving, THEN they attend in person even past the deadline
- GIVEN the deadline has lapsed while still on the road, WHEN resolving, THEN an ambassador takes the seat

### Assemblies/AssemblyConsentPolicyTests.cs

_Documents how a council consents: the stance one member takes on a motion (from regard and personal stake) and whether the council as a whole carries it. The gradient is the point: an advisory council (companions, family) always carries because the clan head decides, while a lords' council can be blocked by its own members._

- GIVEN a motion that costs a cold member, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he opposes
- GIVEN a costly motion but a warmly loyal member, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he still favors it
- GIVEN a motion that helps even an indifferent member, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he favors it
- GIVEN a neutral motion and a lukewarm member, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he abstains
- GIVEN a companions council that all oppose, WHEN tallied, THEN the motion still carries
- GIVEN a clan lords council where opposition outnumbers support, WHEN tallied, THEN it is blocked
- GIVEN a clan lords council split evenly, WHEN tallied, THEN the head breaks the tie and it carries
- GIVEN a clan lords council of only abstentions, WHEN tallied, THEN nothing blocks and it carries
- GIVEN a costly motion and a member exactly at the decent regard floor, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he abstains
- GIVEN a costly motion and a member exactly at warm regard, WHEN he takes a stance, THEN he favors it

### Assemblies/AssemblyDistanceTiersTests.cs

_Documents the distance bucketing: monotonic tiers 0..3, boundaries fall into the higher band, and the tier at Tier3 and beyond is the "far" tier the RSVP relevance gate keys on._

- GIVEN distances in each band, WHEN bucketed, THEN they map to the expected tier
- GIVEN the far tier, WHEN compared to the rsvp gate, THEN they agree that tier 3 is far

### Assemblies/AssemblyLedgerTests.cs

_Documents the assembly ledger: the guarded record the roster panel reads and the executor commits at the lift._

- GIVEN a new motion, WHEN proposed, THEN it is recorded as proposed
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN reversed before the lift, THEN it no longer commits
- GIVEN a merely proposed motion, WHEN reversed, THEN it is withdrawn and commits nothing
- GIVEN a reversed motion, WHEN agreeing it again, THEN the ledger refuses
- GIVEN a mix of agreed and proposed, WHEN asked what commits, THEN only the agreed is offered
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN the bridge fails at the lift, THEN it is marked failed with a reason
- GIVEN an applied motion, WHEN asked to reverse, THEN the ledger refuses
- GIVEN motions on several heroes, WHEN asked for one hero, THEN only his motions return
- GIVEN an unknown id, WHEN agreeing or reversing, THEN it fails without throwing
- GIVEN an id already on the table, WHEN proposing it again, THEN the ledger refuses and keeps the first
- GIVEN a blank id, WHEN proposing, THEN the ledger refuses and records nothing
- GIVEN a target hint, WHEN proposed, THEN it is stored on the entry
- GIVEN a round tripped ledger with blank and duplicate ids, WHEN sanitized, THEN only sound unique entries remain

### Assemblies/AssemblyProgressTests.cs

_Documents the assembly roster tally: which statuses count as a filled seat (Present + Ambassador), which as in transit, and when the roster is fully resolved so the player is no longer waiting on anyone._

- GIVEN a mixed roster, WHEN tallied, THEN each bucket counts the right statuses
- GIVEN someone still awaiting or in transit, WHEN asked, THEN the roster is not all resolved
- GIVEN everyone has landed on a final outcome, WHEN asked, THEN the roster is all resolved
- GIVEN an empty or null roster, WHEN tallied, THEN it is zeroed and trivially resolved

### Assemblies/AssemblyRsvpPolicyTests.cs

_Documents who accepts a summons to an assembly and why they refuse. Order of precedence is load-bearing: availability, then the ground (no one enters an enemy's town), then willingness, then relevance. The thresholds here are the tuning knobs; these tests pin the decision SHAPE, not the exact numbers._

- GIVEN a companion already in the party, WHEN deciding, THEN they are accepted even if far or cool
- GIVEN a companion who is dead or captured, WHEN deciding, THEN availability wins over the shortcut
- GIVEN a dead invitee, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as unavailable
- GIVEN an imprisoned invitee, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as unavailable
- GIVEN an invitee pinned by a siege or battle, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as unavailable
- GIVEN a prisoner on enemy ground, WHEN deciding, THEN availability wins over the ground reason
- GIVEN a host owned by the invitees enemy, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines for enemy ground even when liked
- GIVEN enemy ground and a hostile regard, WHEN deciding, THEN the ground reason wins over hostility
- GIVEN an ally whose regard is below the heed floor, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as hostile
- GIVEN an ally whose regard sits exactly at the heed floor, WHEN deciding, THEN it accepts
- GIVEN an enemy parley with regard between the two floors, WHEN deciding, THEN the lower parley bar lets them come
- GIVEN an enemy parley with regard below even the parley floor, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as hostile
- GIVEN an enemy parley with regard exactly at the parley floor, WHEN deciding, THEN it accepts
- GIVEN a far host and only lukewarm regard, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines as not worth the travel
- GIVEN a far host but genuine regard, WHEN deciding, THEN it accepts the journey
- GIVEN a near host and lukewarm regard, WHEN deciding, THEN the relevance gate does not apply
- GIVEN an available nearby ally with decent regard, WHEN deciding, THEN it accepts
- GIVEN a decision and a mismatched reason, WHEN constructing an outcome, THEN it is refused

### Assemblies/CouncilAccessPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the council/assembly OPEN gate (audit M1/M2). What breaks without it: convening while a chat is already open hijacked that live conversation (its NPC was reassigned as the council anchor and the other members bolted on as witnesses); convening from captivity or a battle scene opened a map gathering where none could exist. This policy is the single tested authority the mod's ChatManager defers to for the verdict._

- GIVEN a free player on the map with no chat open, WHEN evaluated, THEN nothing blocks
- GIVEN a chat already open, WHEN evaluated, THEN it blocks as chat open even over other conditions
- GIVEN a captive player and no chat open, WHEN evaluated, THEN it blocks as captive
- GIVEN a mission scene and a free unengaged player, WHEN evaluated, THEN it blocks as in scene

### Assemblies/CouncilActorPolicyTests.cs

_Decides which seated council member an [ACTION]'s "actor" name refers to, so the action-dispatch loop can execute a non-anchor member's own promise against THEM instead of always the anchor who carries the turn's spoken reply. WHY IT MATTERS: a wrong guess here is worse than dropping the action outright (the wrong hero's gold, the wrong hero married off), so every ambiguous or unrecognized case must resolve to -1, never a guess._

- GIVEN an exact name match, WHEN resolving, THEN that seat is returned
- GIVEN a case difference, WHEN resolving, THEN the seat still matches
- GIVEN a parenthetical role suffix, WHEN resolving, THEN the role is stripped before matching
- GIVEN a first name only match, WHEN resolving, THEN the single fitting seat is returned
- GIVEN an ambiguous fragment matching two members, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN an exact match that is also a substring of another seat, WHEN resolving, THEN the exact seat wins
- GIVEN an actor naming nobody seated, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a null or blank actor, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused without throwing
- GIVEN no seated members, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused without throwing
- GIVEN a blank actor, WHEN resolving a declare war actor, THEN the presiding seat is returned
- GIVEN a named actor, WHEN resolving a declare war actor, THEN it resolves like any other kind
- GIVEN an unmatched named actor, WHEN resolving a declare war actor, THEN it is still refused
- GIVEN no seated members, WHEN resolving a declare war actor, THEN it is refused without throwing
- GIVEN a blank actor, WHEN resolving a make peace actor, THEN the presiding seat is returned
- GIVEN a named actor, WHEN resolving a make peace actor, THEN it resolves like any other kind
- GIVEN an unmatched named actor, WHEN resolving a make peace actor, THEN it is still refused
- GIVEN no seated members, WHEN resolving a make peace actor, THEN it is refused without throwing

### Assemblies/CouncilAttendancePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the single decision of how one candidate hero shows up at a council: In Person, Ambassador, or Absent. The player complained the war council felt magic (distant kingdom nobility appearing in person); if a far own-faction lord resolved to InPerson or Ambassador instead of Absent, the whole point of the convergence (Council + Assembly agreeing on ONE seating rule) is lost. Order is load-bearing: Prisoner, then InParty, then ParleyTarget, then own-faction willing/near, then everything else is Absent._

- GIVEN a prisoner who is also in the players party and own faction, WHEN deciding, THEN prisoner wins and they are absent
- GIVEN a hero in the players party, WHEN deciding, THEN they attend in person even if their own faction is far
- GIVEN an other faction hero who is in the players party, WHEN deciding, THEN party co location still wins
- GIVEN an own faction lord whose eta sits exactly at the threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN they attend in person
- GIVEN an own faction lord whose eta is just over the threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN they are absent as too far
- GIVEN a far own faction lord, WHEN deciding, THEN they are absent and never an ambassador
- GIVEN a nearby own faction lord at open war with the player, WHEN deciding, THEN they are absent as declined
- GIVEN a nearby own faction lord whose regard sits exactly at the hostile floor, WHEN deciding, THEN they are absent as declined
- GIVEN a nearby own faction lord whose regard is just above the hostile floor, WHEN deciding, THEN they attend in person
- GIVEN a parley target, WHEN deciding, THEN they are represented by an ambassador regardless of distance or war
- GIVEN an other faction hero not sought for a parley, WHEN deciding, THEN they are absent as not invited

### Assemblies/CouncilPledgeMemoryTests.cs

_Documents the council pledge memory (audit M7). What breaks without it: a member who pledged at the council and then withdrew, or whose pledge could not be carried out, kept NO memory of it, so next time the player spoke to them they had forgotten a promise the whole table heard. These pin that every outcome yields a first-person line that names what was pledged, so the promise is remembered whatever became of it._

- GIVEN a withdrawn pledge, WHEN remembered, THEN it reads as a word taken back
- GIVEN a failed pledge, WHEN remembered, THEN it reads as having come to nothing
- GIVEN a kept pledge, WHEN remembered, THEN it reads as made good
- GIVEN a blank detail, WHEN remembered, THEN it falls back to a neutral phrase

### Assemblies/CouncilQuestTypePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the council quest_type grounding (player report 2026-08-25: eight siege-council pledges all grounded as bandit-clear tasks on the same settlement, so clearing ONE bandit camp stamped the whole table's quests "Done" at once). The policy decides WHICH deed a pledge grounds as; the codec round-trips that decision through the ledger entry's single TargetHint slot, legacy bare-settlement entries included._

- GIVEN each teachable token, WHEN resolved, THEN it maps to its own deed type
- GIVEN the teachable list, WHEN resolved, THEN every token grounds as its own deed
- GIVEN no token, WHEN resolved, THEN the legacy bandit clear grounding is kept
- GIVEN an unrecognized token, WHEN resolved, THEN it falls back to bandit clear
- GIVEN a recognized but hero bound token, WHEN resolved, THEN it falls back to bandit clear
- GIVEN a synonym token, WHEN resolved, THEN the sdk vocabulary applies
- GIVEN a type and settlement, WHEN composed then decomposed, THEN both halves survive
- GIVEN no quest type, WHEN composed, THEN the bare legacy settlement is written
- GIVEN a legacy bare settlement, WHEN decomposed, THEN the type is null and the place whole
- GIVEN a blank hint, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails

### Assemblies/CouncilResolutionPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the two session-ledger decisions the [RESOLUTION] channel needs on top of AssemblyLedger and ResolutionLifecycle: whether a new pledge may be recorded (generalising QuestService's own "one task at a time" rule to the council), and which of a member's live pledges a bare "type: withdraw" targets, since the block carries no resolution id and the owner rejected a manual delete button. Both share CouncilActorPolicy's discipline: an ambiguous case is refused, never guessed._

- GIVEN no existing entries, WHEN recording, THEN it is allowed
- GIVEN the perception verb, WHEN asking if it runs at a council, THEN it executes immediately
- GIVEN a world transfer or commitment verb, WHEN asking if it runs at a council, THEN it is deferred
- GIVEN a blank action type, WHEN asking if it runs at a council, THEN it is not immediate
- GIVEN a live pledge of the same kind, WHEN recording another, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a withdrawn pledge of the same kind, WHEN recording another, THEN it is allowed
- GIVEN a live pledge of a different kind, WHEN recording, THEN it is allowed
- GIVEN exactly one live entry, WHEN selecting a withdraw target, THEN it is returned
- GIVEN no live entries, WHEN selecting a withdraw target, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN two live entries, WHEN the detail matches only one, THEN that one is returned
- GIVEN two live entries, WHEN the detail cannot disambiguate, THEN it is refused

### Assemblies/CouncilRosterPolicyTests.cs

_Documents who is seated at a clan council: availability bars first, then membership in the chosen roster. This feeds the roster panel on the right of the council screen (who shows up, and why an absent kinsman is absent)._

- GIVEN a companion, WHEN a companions council is gathered, THEN he is seated
- GIVEN a companion, WHEN a clan lords council is gathered, THEN he is not of that council
- GIVEN a sibling lord, WHEN either council is gathered, THEN he is seated at both
- GIVEN an imprisoned family member, WHEN a family council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as imprisoned
- GIVEN a clan lord in a battle, WHEN a council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as in battle
- GIVEN a dead member, WHEN any council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as dead
- GIVEN a clan lord in an army, WHEN a council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as in army
- GIVEN a companion away on a mission, WHEN a companions council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as on mission
- GIVEN a dead member who is also flagged in an army, WHEN a council is gathered, THEN dead still wins
- GIVEN a prisoner who is also flagged away on a mission, WHEN a council is gathered, THEN imprisoned still wins
- GIVEN a member in battle who is also flagged in an army, WHEN a council is gathered, THEN in battle still wins
- GIVEN an allied leader, WHEN a war council is gathered, THEN he is seated
- GIVEN a clan lord who is not an allied leader, WHEN a war council is gathered, THEN he is not of that council
- GIVEN an allied leader in an army, WHEN a war council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as in army
- GIVEN an allied leader held prisoner, WHEN a war council is gathered, THEN he is excluded as imprisoned
- GIVEN the parley counterpart, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is seated
- GIVEN an allied leader who is not the parley counterpart, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is not of that council
- GIVEN the parley counterpart leading an army, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is still seated
- GIVEN the parley counterpart in battle, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is still seated
- GIVEN the parley counterpart flagged on mission, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is still seated
- GIVEN a dead parley counterpart, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is excluded as dead
- GIVEN a captured parley counterpart, WHEN a parley is gathered, THEN he is excluded as imprisoned
- GIVEN a heros age, WHEN asked if old enough to sit, THEN only the threshold and up qualify
- GIVEN the seat age boundary, WHEN checked, THEN it is inclusive at MinSeatAge
- GIVEN a party member, WHEN a party council is gathered, THEN they are seated
- GIVEN a party member in a battle, WHEN a party council is gathered, THEN they are excluded as in battle

### Assemblies/CouncilTroopDeliveryTests.cs

_The mod's CouncilTroopDeliveryBehavior persists a List<CouncilTroopDelivery> as byte[] JSON via Newtonsoft.Json, mirroring CompanionMissionBehavior's and CouncilStipendBehavior's own SyncData shape exactly (idempotent load, graceful on missing/corrupt, see CouncilStipendTests.cs's own header for why the mod project itself is integration-only here). What IS unit-testable, and IS the real save contract the behavior depends on, is the DTO's own round trip through the same serializer: a delegation is a real party physically travelling the map with someone else's troops already loaded aboard it (Gabriel's condition B, a PERMANENT gift once it departs) - a save made mid-journey corrupting any one of these fields would either strand the escort forever (lost PartyId) or misattribute/miscount the troops on arrival (lost tier counts or donor id)._

- GIVEN an in flight delivery with an escort leader, WHEN round tripped through the same serializer the behavior uses, THEN every field survives
- GIVEN a delivery with no escort leader recorded, WHEN round tripped, THEN the null survives

### Assemblies/CouncilTroopLoanPolicyTests.cs

_CouncilTroopLoanPolicy decides HOW MANY troops, split by tier, a lord's party parts with for a council lend_troops resolution. Gabriel's own written CONDITIONS (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md) are authoritative and are what these tests pin: (C) "jamais plus de 25% du party du Lord. Doit avoir une distribution de tiers avec faible, moyen, haut" - a hard 25% ceiling AND a genuine tier mix, never a pure levy dump, whenever the party actually fields higher tiers. Get the cap wrong and a council pledge could gut a lord's real fighting strength (or a battle-deciding windfall for the player); get the mix wrong and the promise reads as "troops" while every soldier delivered is an unarmoured levy, the exact LLM-says/game-does gap this whole project exists to close._

- GIVEN a pure low tier party of various sizes, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it never exceeds 25 percent
- GIVEN a party too small to spare a quarter, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends nothing
- GIVEN an empty party, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends nothing
- GIVEN a mostly low tier party with a thin sliver of medium, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN at least one medium troop is included
- GIVEN a mostly low tier party with a thin sliver of high tier, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN at least one high tier troop is included
- GIVEN a party with a clean proportional mix, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the split matches the proportions
- GIVEN a party split evenly across all three tiers, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the loan is also even
- GIVEN a party dominated by its high tier, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the dominant tier is represented
- GIVEN a party with only one tier present, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends only from that tier up to the cap
- GIVEN a bucket constructed with a negative count, WHEN read back, THEN it clamps to zero
- GIVEN a wide spread of party shapes, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it never exceeds the cap or any tiers own count

### Assemblies/CouncilVenuePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the venue pick for a settlement-hosted council: which interior the player actually walks into when convening. Wrong here means the player either gets locked out of a settlement that DOES have a usable room, or gets shoved into a hall they do not own, which would read as a bug in the "reuse the proven duel scene" recipe._

- GIVEN the player owns the settlement and it has a lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the hall is chosen
- GIVEN the player is a guest and the settlement has a tavern, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the tavern is chosen
- GIVEN the player owns the settlement but it has no lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the tavern is chosen as fallback
- GIVEN the player is a guest and the settlement has no tavern but has a lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the hall is chosen as fallback
- GIVEN the settlement has neither a lordshall nor a tavern, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN each venue, WHEN asked for its location id, THEN the engine ids map correctly

### Assemblies/CouncilWardTests.cs

_The mod's CouncilWardBehavior persists a List<CouncilWard> as byte[] JSON via Newtonsoft.Json, mirroring CouncilStipendBehavior's/CouncilTributeBehavior's/CouncilHostageBehavior's own SyncData shape exactly (idempotent load, graceful on missing/corrupt). The mod project (CampaignBehaviorBase, IDataStore, Hero) is not referenced by this test project, so the behavior's actual SyncData call is integration-only (see project_testing_architecture: mod = engine, untestable here). What IS unit-testable, and IS the real save contract the behavior depends on, is the DTO's own round trip through the same serializer: these tests pin that a bond's mentor/protege ids survive a save/load cycle intact, since a save made mid-bond corrupting either id would silently orphan the mentorship (or worse, misattribute it to the wrong pair of heroes)._

- GIVEN an active bond, WHEN round tripped through the same serializer the behavior uses, THEN every field survives
- GIVEN no active bonds, WHEN round tripped, THEN it is an empty list not null
- GIVEN corrupt json, WHEN deserialized, THEN it throws so the behaviors own catch path can fall back

### Assemblies/EngagementLedgerTests.cs

_The engagements ledger is the live MIRROR of a council conversation's standing decisions: what the right-hand column shows, and what applies to the world when the council lifts. It has no cancel button by design (Gabriel ruled that an immersion break, 2026-07-30); a decision is walked back purely by the conversation no longer asserting it, so the extraction snapshot drops it and this ledger must drop it too. What breaks if reconcile is wrong: a decision the table rescinded would still be on the panel and would still apply at the lift (the exact dishonesty the "player sees it, then it applies" rule exists to prevent), or a decision restated each turn would pile up as duplicate rows. Gabriel asked specifically for the appears-then-retraction pair pinned here._

- GIVEN a declaration in the snapshot, WHEN reconciling, THEN it appears in the ledger
- GIVEN a standing declaration, WHEN a later snapshot no longer carries it, THEN it is removed
- GIVEN the same declaration across two snapshots, WHEN reconciling twice, THEN it is not duplicated
- GIVEN a discussed declaration, WHEN it becomes agreed in a later snapshot, THEN its status moves in place
- GIVEN a mix of discussed and agreed, WHEN asking what commits, THEN only the agreed ones are returned
- GIVEN snapshot items with no key or no text, WHEN reconciling, THEN they are ignored

### Assemblies/EngagementSealTextTests.cs

_Documents the durable memory wording the Engagements apply-at-End seals onto a hero at council close. Real world-effect executors do not exist yet for a council engagement (only a bandit-clear quest is executable elsewhere in this codebase), so this phrasing IS the applied effect: what breaks if it drifts is either a decision misattributed as the player's own promise when it was collective, or the reverse._

- GIVEN a decision bound to a seated subject, WHEN sealed, THEN it reads in the first person
- GIVEN a decision with no bound subject, WHEN sealed, THEN it reads as a collective settlement
- GIVEN blank text, WHEN sealed, THEN it returns empty
- GIVEN text already ending in a period, WHEN sealed, THEN the period is not doubled

### Assemblies/EngagementSnapshotParserTests.cs

_The parser is the ONLY thing standing between raw LLM output and EngagementLedger.Reconcile: the ledger mirror, and thus what applies to the world at the lift, is only as honest as this parse. A throw here would kill a live council turn; a mis-mapped status would apply a decision the table never actually settled; a lost well-formed head to a malformed tail would make the panel flicker away decisions the player just watched form._

- GIVEN a clean json array of two items, WHEN parsing, THEN both map to snapshot items
- GIVEN a markdown fenced array, WHEN parsing, THEN it still parses
- GIVEN prose surrounding the array, WHEN parsing, THEN the array is still located and parsed
- GIVEN a status string, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps case insensitively and defaults to discussed
- GIVEN an element missing id or with blank decision, WHEN parsing, THEN it is skipped
- GIVEN null or blank input, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN input that is not json, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN a truncated array missing its closing bracket, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN a well formed head followed by a malformed element, WHEN parsing, THEN the head survives and nothing throws
- GIVEN a malformed array with no well formed head, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned

### Assemblies/EngagementSubjectMatcherTests.cs

_Documents the tolerant subject-to-seat match the Engagements apply-at-End seal depends on. What breaks without it: a wrong match seals a decision onto the wrong hero's durable memory, so an NPC would recall a promise that was never theirs. Returning -1 for an unresolved or collective subject is equally load-bearing, since that is what routes the seal to the presiding anchor instead of guessing a seated member._

- GIVEN a subject naming a single seated member, WHEN matching, THEN that members index is returned
- GIVEN a seated name inside a longer subject, WHEN matching, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN a subject naming nobody seated, WHEN matching, THEN no match is returned
- GIVEN a blank subject or no participants, WHEN matching, THEN no match is returned
- GIVEN a subject that could match two seated members, WHEN matching, THEN the first wins

### Assemblies/EnvoyIdentityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the wording rule for a synthetic ambassador's identity: the display name and persona line the player and the LLM actually see. The player report this whole convergence traces back to is a council that "felt magic" (a stand-in appearing as if it were the real lord); if the display name or persona ever failed to name the represented faction/leader, or leaked a raw "null"/empty placeholder from a missing name, the seat would either read as the wrong person or as visibly broken. Both DisplayName and Persona are pure string composition, so every boundary (blank, null, both present) is exercised here rather than left to be noticed in a live scene._

- GIVEN a faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it reads herald of that faction
- GIVEN a faction name with surrounding whitespace, WHEN building the display name, THEN it is trimmed
- GIVEN a null faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it degrades to the generic fallback without emitting null
- GIVEN a blank faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it degrades to the generic fallback
- GIVEN a faction and leader name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it names both and states it binds nothing
- GIVEN a faction name but no leader name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades without emitting null or a double space
- GIVEN a leader name but no faction name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades without emitting null
- GIVEN both faction and leader name missing, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades to a generic but coherent sentence

### Assemblies/ExpulsionPolicyTests.cs

_Documents expel_from_clan's pure gate: whether the player may cast a seated companion out of their own clan right now. The stake across every test here: expulsion is one of the gravest social costs the table can hand down (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, Partie 8), so getting the AND wrong either lets a non-leader force out a companion that was never theirs to dismiss, exposes a marriage-ending loophole through the wrong channel, or refuses a legal expulsion the lift should have carried out._

- GIVEN a clan leader and a live companion, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not a companion, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is the players own spouse, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not alive, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN no condition holds, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the expulsion tuning constants, WHEN read, THEN they match the documented design
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the target is the players own spouse, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the companion is away on an errand, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/FiefSwapGroundingCodecTests.cs

_Documents the composite grounding swap_fiefs uses to carry TWO fief names through the single TargetHint slot every other resolution kind reuses, mirroring ArrangeMarriageGroundingCodecTests' own coverage. The stake throughout: a codec that fails to round-trip could leave CouncilLift.SettleSwapFiefs resolving the wrong pair of fiefs, or worse, transferring one side of the trade while silently dropping the other, exactly the half-a-swap outcome the whole atomic design exists to prevent._

- GIVEN two ordinary fief names, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN both survive unchanged
- GIVEN a null or blank composite, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a composite with no delimiter, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN either half is blank, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN the target fief name itself contains the delimiter, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it survives whole
- GIVEN a null half, WHEN composed, THEN it does not throw and still fails to decompose

### Assemblies/FiefTransferPolicyTests.cs

_Documents grant_fief/revoke_fief's pure gate: whether a fief transfer between the crown and a vassal is currently legal. A fief is CLAN-owned in vanilla, never hero-owned, so the stake across every test here is the same one COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review pins: only a true kingdom sovereign may move one at all, and only when the named fief is actually where the pledge says it is (the player's own clan for a grant, the spoliated vassal's own clan for a revoke). Getting either AND wrong either lets a non-sovereign clan leader hand out or seize a fief that was never theirs to move, or refuses a legal transfer the lift should have carried out._

- GIVEN a sovereign and a player owned fief, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the recipient is not a vassal of the players kingdom, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named fief is not the players own, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN neither condition holds, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a sovereign and a vassal owned fief, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not a vassal of the players kingdom, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named fief is not the vassals own, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN neither condition holds, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the revoke tuning constants, WHEN read, THEN they match the documented design
- GIVEN the grant influence cost, WHEN read, THEN it matches the documented design
- GIVEN a sovereign and both fiefs owned as expected, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named player fief is not the players own, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named vassal fief is not the vassals own, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN any single condition is missing, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN every grant teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not rule a kingdom, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is not a vassal in the players kingdom, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no grantable crown fief, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN every revoke teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not rule a kingdom, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is not a vassal in the players kingdom, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no revocable fief, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/GiveItemPolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_item's pure policy: the one rule that decides whether an item may ever be given away at all._

- GIVEN an item with a positive value, WHEN checked, THEN it is giveable
- GIVEN an item with a zero value, WHEN checked, THEN it is not giveable
- GIVEN an item with a negative value, WHEN checked, THEN it is not giveable
- GIVEN an item worth exactly one denar, WHEN checked, THEN it is giveable

### Assemblies/GoldPledgePolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_gold's pure policy: the payable-amount clamp mirroring the existing 1:1 give_gold action's own solvency rule (BannerlordGameStateBridge.ExecuteGiveGold). The stake across every test here is the same: a council pledge must never conjure coin the giver does not hold, and a pledge from an insolvent giver must be refused rather than silently applied for zero denars while still reporting success._

- GIVEN a proposed amount the giver can afford, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN a proposed amount above the givers gold, WHEN clamped, THEN it is capped to their gold
- GIVEN a proposed amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it is capped to the maximum
- GIVEN a giver with no gold, WHEN clamped, THEN the payable amount is zero
- GIVEN a non positive proposed amount, WHEN clamped, THEN the payable amount is zero
- GIVEN a proposed amount exactly at a boundary, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged

### Assemblies/GovernorAppointmentPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the FIRST governance executor's own refusal rule (CouncilLift.SettleAppointGovernor). Author's condition: appointing a companion or clan lord as governor is a real pledge the lift may carry out, and the prospective governor MAY refuse it - but only MECHANICALLY, from their own standing toward the player, never as a narrated refusal invented after ChangeGovernorAction already ran. That is exactly the phantom-deed class this project eliminates elsewhere (a quest "completed" with no verified deed, a relation gain with no gate): here the failure mode would be a governor appointed in the save data while the story tells the player they were turned down, or the reverse. Getting the boundary wrong in either direction reopens that class of bug._

- GIVEN high standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN neutral standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN a mildly negative standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is still accepted
- GIVEN deep resentment, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is refused
- GIVEN standing one point above the resentment floor, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is not of the players clan, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is unavailable, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no vacant governable fief, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee already governs another fief, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is away on an errand, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/HostageEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_hostage's eligibility gate: COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own five CONDITIONS ("vivant, libre, majeur, pas chef de clan, pas heritier unique"), each pinned by its own test so a future change to the predicate cannot silently drop one of Gabriel's own guards. The STAKE across every test here: an ineligible relative (a minor, an imprisoned kinsman, a clan chief, or the pledger's clan's own sole remaining heir) offered as a hostage would either be mechanically wrong (a chief has no business residing at a foreign court) or would gamble away a clan's own succession, exactly the "enjeu de succession vanilla trop lourd" Gabriel's own condition warns against._

- GIVEN every condition met, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is
- GIVEN the relative is dead, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is not free, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is a minor, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is a clan chief, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is the clans sole heir, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN multiple conditions fail at once, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is still refused

### Assemblies/HostagePolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_hostage's ONLY pure policy figure: the fixed 45-day residency term. Kimi's "v1 invite d'honneur" (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Partie 8) deliberately fixes the term rather than letting the LLM negotiate a second parameter alongside the relative's own name, mirroring TributePolicy.TributeDays'/StipendPolicy.TrancheDays' own simplification. The STAKE across the single test here: a future tuning pass cannot silently drift this figure out of COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own 20-90 day range without a test noticing._

- GIVEN the hostage term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed forty five days
- GIVEN the hostage term, WHEN asked, THEN it sits within the documented twenty to ninety day range

### Assemblies/MemberPresenceDescriberTests.cs

_Documents the phrase CouncilRosterResolver attaches to each seated member so PromptBuilder's council block can state the truth of where they stand. STAKE (author's bug report, "bug c"): CouncilRosterPolicy seats a member on availability alone, by design with no distance test (see its own header), so a companion staying behind can be seated without ever being in the player's party; without this phrase and the directive it feeds, that companion could then claim in dialogue to be "already at your side" on the road. Every case here is a real seated-but-not-in-party shape the mod must describe honestly instead of leaving the model to guess._

- GIVEN a member riding in the players party, WHEN describing, THEN at your side in your party
- GIVEN a member leading their own party, WHEN describing, THEN leading their own company
- GIVEN a member staying in a named settlement, WHEN describing, THEN keeping to that place
- GIVEN a member with a blank settlement name, WHEN describing, THEN the generic abroad phrase
- GIVEN a member with none of the above, WHEN describing, THEN abroad not travelling with you
- GIVEN conflicting stale facts, WHEN describing, THEN in main party wins
- GIVEN leading their own party and a stale settlement name, WHEN describing, THEN leading wins

### Assemblies/MissionTypeParserTests.cs

_MissionTypeParser is the only bridge between what the LLM writes in a [RESOLUTION]'s target_mission field (carried through AssemblyResolutionEntry.TargetHint, the same single grounding slot assign_party_role uses for a party role) and the real CompanionMissionBehavior errand CouncilLift.SettleDispatchMission dispatches. The stake: a mission type must be resolved from a STRUCTURED value, never prose-parsed at the lift, and an unrecognised type must fail cleanly (None) rather than guess an errand the model did not actually name, or a companion could be sent on an errand nobody asked for._

- GIVEN a recognised mission name, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the matching type
- GIVEN mixed case, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN a legacy gather news errand word, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the merged type
- GIVEN blank or unrecognised text, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to none

### Assemblies/NonAggressionPactPolicyTests.cs

_Documents non_aggression_pact's pure policy: the fixed term, the order-independent faction-pair match (CouncilPactBehavior.IsUnderPact's own core), and the breach-fault predicates a real MapEvent's attacker/ defender sides feed into (CouncilPactBehavior.OnMapEventEnded). The STAKE across every test here: this pact is HONEST-LIMITED by design (see NonAggressionPactPolicy's own header) - it binds only the mod's own hostile-quest emission and a detected breach, never vanilla AI, so the fault-direction rule (a grudge ONLY when the player's own side attacks) must never be inverted, or the mod would plant a nonsensical grudge (an NPC resenting the player for the NPC'S OWN faction's attack)._

- GIVEN the pact term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed forty five days
- GIVEN the breach consequence figures, WHEN asked, THEN they match the doc
- GIVEN the same pair in either order, WHEN matched, THEN both orders are recognized
- GIVEN a mismatched pair, WHEN matched, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a null or blank id on either side, WHEN matched, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN factionA attacking factionB, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is a breach
- GIVEN factionB attacking factionA, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is still a breach
- GIVEN the battle does not pit the two pact factions against each other, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is not a breach
- GIVEN factionA is the attacker, WHEN asking who is at fault, THEN the player is at fault
- GIVEN factionA is not the attacker, WHEN asking who is at fault, THEN the player is not at fault

### Assemblies/OathGroundingCodecTests.cs

_Documents the composite grounding swear_oath uses to carry (oath kind, target value) through the single TargetHint slot every other resolution kind reuses, mirroring ArrangeMarriageGroundingCodecTests'/ FiefSwapGroundingCodecTests' own coverage. The ONE deliberate difference from those two: this codec's RIGHT half (the target value) may legitimately be blank (a protect oath names no target beyond the player), so its own round-trip rule differs and needs its own tests, not just a copy of theirs. The stake throughout: a codec that fails to round-trip could leave CouncilLift.SettleSwearOath tracking the WRONG oath kind, or refusing a perfectly valid protect oath because its target half is empty._

- GIVEN an oath kind and a target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN both survive unchanged
- GIVEN a blank target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it still succeeds with an empty target
- GIVEN a null target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it still succeeds with an empty target
- GIVEN a blank oath kind, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails even if a target value is present
- GIVEN a null or blank composite, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a composite with no delimiter, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN the target value itself contains the delimiter, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it survives whole

### Assemblies/OathOfferPolicyTests.cs

_OathOfferPolicy is the 1:1 conversation TEACHING gate for swear_oath, brought to the 1:1 pipeline alongside the council on 2026-08-05. What it decides: whether the LLM is even SHOWN the swear_oath action this turn, from the plain engine facts the mod's EncounterContextBuilder reads about the ONE conversation NPC. What it feeds: PromptBuilder.AppendSwearOathOffer's gate. What breaks if it is wrong: it guards the empty-promise class the whole conditional-teaching contract exists to prevent. It mirrors the council's own swearer eligibility (ChatViewModel.AnySeatedMemberCanSwearOath): alive, free, not the player, no oath already outstanding (one vow at a time), and plausibly qualifying for at least one whitelisted oath kind. Every fact is re-checked at the bridge (CourtActionResolver.TrySwearOath), so a true answer here only ever OFFERS a teaching, never records an oath. Each fact below removes one way an NPC could otherwise "agree" to an oath the game then silently declines._

- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering an oath, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the npc is dead, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is imprisoned, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is the player, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc already carries an oath, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc qualifies for no oath kind, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld

### Assemblies/PartyCommandOfferPolicyTests.cs

_PartyCommandOfferPolicy is the 1:1 conversation TEACHING gate for the three party-command verbs (assign_party_role, rejoin_party, dispatch_mission) brought to the 1:1 pipeline alongside the council on 2026-08-05. What it decides: whether the LLM is even SHOWN each action this turn, from the plain engine facts the mod's EncounterContextBuilder reads about the ONE conversation NPC. What it feeds: PromptBuilder's Append*Offer gates. What breaks if it is wrong: it guards the empty-promise class the whole conditional-teaching contract exists to prevent. Each gate mirrors the council's own offering eligibility for its verb (ResolutionOfferingResolver: the TargetInPlayerParty fact for assign/dispatch, the TargetOutOfPlayerParty fact for rejoin), so teach a verb only when the bridge's own re-check (CourtActionResolver.TryAssignPartyRole / TryRejoinParty / TryDispatchMission) could actually honour it, and an NPC can never "agree" to something the game then silently declines. Each fact below removes one way that could happen._

- GIVEN a companion in the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion out of the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a non companion in the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an away free companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion already in the party, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an imprisoned companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a companion leading their own company, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a dead companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an available companion in the party, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion not free to ride out, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a companion out of the party, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN someone who cannot ride for the player, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld

_PartyRoleParser is the only bridge between what the LLM writes in a [RESOLUTION]'s target_role field (carried through AssemblyResolutionEntry.TargetHint, the same single grounding slot appoint_governor uses for a fief name) and the real engine role CouncilLift.SettleAssignPartyRole assigns. The stake: a role must be resolved from a STRUCTURED value, never prose-parsed at the lift, and an unrecognised role must fail cleanly (None) rather than guess a role the model did not actually name, or a companion could be assigned a role nobody asked for._

- GIVEN a recognised role name, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the matching role
- GIVEN mixed case, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN blank or unrecognised text, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to none

### Assemblies/PledgeAgainstPolicyTests.cs

_Documents pledge_against's one council-specific caveat: a member must not be allowed to scheme against a rival seated at the very same table (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, "trahison a table"). The stake across every test here is the same: getting this wrong either lets the table launder a public betrayal it never priced, or wrongly refuses a legitimate pledge against someone who was never actually present._

- GIVEN the rival was not seated at this council, WHEN checked, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN a null or empty witness list, WHEN checked, THEN it is never flagged
- GIVEN a null or blank target id, WHEN checked, THEN it is never flagged

### Assemblies/ResolutionEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents which catalogue motions a council may actually offer given the world. The rule is AND: every condition a motion requires must hold. The stake is the empty-promise guard, if a motion is offered while a condition is unmet (no vacant fief, no gold), the council could pass a resolution the bridge cannot apply, which the whole verification design exists to prevent._

- GIVEN assign party role, WHEN the target is or is not in the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN dispatch mission, WHEN the target is or is not in the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN rejoin party, WHEN the target is or is not out of the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN appoint governor, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN grant stipend, WHEN the player cannot afford it, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a companions council and partial facts, WHEN offered, THEN only the satisfied motions appear
- GIVEN a vacant fief and an available member, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN appoint governor is included
- GIVEN no vacant fief OR no available member, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN appoint governor is withheld
- GIVEN a seated companion out of the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN rejoin party is included
- GIVEN no seated companion out of the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN rejoin party is withheld
- GIVEN a seated member in the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN dispatch mission is included
- GIVEN no seated member in the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN dispatch mission is withheld
- GIVEN the player can afford the floor tranche, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant stipend is included
- GIVEN the player cannot afford the floor tranche, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant stipend is withheld
- GIVEN any scope and empty facts, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN quest is always included
- GIVEN the player can declare war, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN declare war is included
- GIVEN the player cannot declare war, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN declare war is withheld
- GIVEN the player can make peace, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN make peace is included
- GIVEN the player cannot make peace, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN make peace is withheld
- GIVEN give gold, WHEN a member does or does not hold gold, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated clan lord holds gold, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is included
- GIVEN no seated war council member holds gold, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is withheld
- GIVEN a companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is never included
- GIVEN give influence, WHEN a member can or cannot grant influence, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated ally can grant influence, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is included
- GIVEN no seated ally can grant influence, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is never included
- GIVEN grant fief, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can grant a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is included
- GIVEN the player cannot grant a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is never included
- GIVEN revoke fief, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can revoke a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is included
- GIVEN the player cannot revoke a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is never included
- GIVEN expel from clan, WHEN no seated companion is expellable, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a companion is expellable, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is included
- GIVEN no companion is expellable, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is withheld
- GIVEN a family clan lords war council or parley, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is never included
- GIVEN pledge against, WHEN no seated member can pledge, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated member can pledge against a rival, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is included
- GIVEN no seated member can pledge against a rival, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is withheld
- GIVEN a companions war council or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is never included
- GIVEN arrange marriage, WHEN no seated ally offers a marriageable kin, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated ally offers a marriageable kin, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is included
- GIVEN no seated ally offers a marriageable kin, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is never included
- GIVEN swap fiefs, WHEN any condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can swap a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is included
- GIVEN the player cannot swap a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is never included
- GIVEN tribute, WHEN no seated counterpart can pay, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated counterpart can pay tribute, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is included
- GIVEN no seated counterpart can pay tribute, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is never included
- GIVEN release prisoner, WHEN the player does or does not hold a prisoner of the enemy faction, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN the player holds a prisoner of the enemy faction, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is included
- GIVEN the player holds no prisoner of the enemy faction, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is never included
- GIVEN lend troops, WHEN any seated member can or cannot lend troops, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord can lend troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is included
- GIVEN no seated member can lend troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is never included
- GIVEN give troops, WHEN any seated lord can or cannot receive troops, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord can receive troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is included
