- GIVEN no seated lord can receive troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is never included
- GIVEN non aggression pact, WHEN a seated counterpart is or is not available, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated counterpart can pact, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is included
- GIVEN no seated counterpart can pact, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is never included
- GIVEN give item, WHEN any seated lord has or has not a giveable item, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord has a giveable item, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is included
- GIVEN no seated lord has a giveable item, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is never included
- GIVEN support claimant, WHEN a member can or cannot support the players claim, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated vassal can support the players claim, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is included
- GIVEN no seated vassal can support the players claim, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is never included

### Assemblies/ResolutionLifecycleTests.cs

_Documents the assembly-motion transaction model: what is provisional, what can be cancelled, and what actually commits when the assembly is lifted. The cancellation path is the one that MUST be solid: a player who takes back a decision mid-council must leave no trace in the world, because nothing was applied before the lift._

- GIVEN a proposed motion, WHEN asked, THEN it is provisional and can be reversed
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN asked before the lift, THEN it is still provisional and reversible
- GIVEN a reversed motion, WHEN asked, THEN it is terminal and cannot transition anywhere
- GIVEN the lift, WHEN deciding what commits, THEN only agreed motions do
- GIVEN a proposed motion, WHEN it tries to commit directly, THEN the transition is illegal
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN committed at the lift, THEN it ends as applied or failed and stops
- GIVEN an applied or failed motion, WHEN asked to reverse, THEN it refuses

### Assemblies/StipendClosurePolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.2 companion hardening. StipendClosurePolicy decides how CouncilStipendBehavior.Advance ENDS a running stipend each day: keep paying, close a completed tranche, or refund a dead payee's unspent escrow. Before this, the behavior removed a dead payee's stipend with no refund and no notice, silently swallowing up to the whole remaining escrow (~15k denars possible) that had been financed from the PLAYER's own purse up front; and a normal completion ended with only a log line the player never saw. These tests pin the classification and the refund amount, since a wrong answer either strands the player's already-spent gold or ends an escrow unseen._

- GIVEN the payee is gone mid tranche, WHEN classified, THEN it is PayeeGone so the escrow is refunded not swallowed
- GIVEN a live payee with days and escrow left, WHEN classified, THEN it continues
- GIVEN a live payee whose days have elapsed, WHEN classified, THEN it is Completed
- GIVEN a live payee whose escrow is empty, WHEN classified, THEN it is Completed
- GIVEN a payee gone with escrow left, WHEN sizing the refund, THEN it is the whole unpaid remainder
- GIVEN a non positive escrow, WHEN sizing the refund, THEN it is zero never negative

### Assemblies/StipendPolicyTests.cs

_Documents grant_stipend's pure policy: the daily-amount clamp, the escrow total, and the afford gate both the offering (at the floor, before any amount is proposed) and the lift (at the real proposed amount) read. The stake across every test here is the same: a promise that outruns the player's purse must be refused before the lift ever touches the world, never applied partially and never left to fail silently mid-term (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md "grant_stipend", ESCROW model - Kimi review, adopted as-is)._

- GIVEN an amount below the floor, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the minimum
- GIVEN an amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the maximum
- GIVEN an amount within range, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN an amount per day, WHEN the total cost is asked, THEN it is the amount times the tranche
- GIVEN the players gold, WHEN afford is asked, THEN it requires covering the whole tranche
- GIVEN the minimum tranche cost, WHEN computed, THEN it matches the floor rate over the full tranche
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the npc is not a player companion, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a stipend already runs for the npc, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the player cannot afford the floor tranche, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld

### Assemblies/TributePolicyTests.cs

_Documents tribute's pure policy: the daily-amount clamp, the fixed 30-day term, and the pay-as-you-go afford check read fresh every tick. The stake across every test here: a tribute that outpaces the payer's own purse must degrade gracefully day by day (CanPay says no, the behavior tolerates it up to the grace threshold), it must never be able to clamp the payer's gold negative, and the amount itself must never reach the lift unclamped, however the LLM proposed it (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, "tribute")._

- GIVEN an amount below the floor, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the minimum
- GIVEN an amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the maximum
- GIVEN an amount within range, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN the payers gold, WHEN asked if todays tribute is payable, THEN it only needs to cover one day
- GIVEN a payer with no gold, WHEN asked if tribute is payable, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the tribute term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed thirty days
- GIVEN the grace threshold, WHEN asked, THEN it is three consecutive missed days

### Assemblies/WarCouncilTriggerPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the war-council OFFER gate. What breaks without it: the player suggestion is that after an army forms or wins a considerable action, the mod OFFERS to convene the war council. This policy is the single tested authority WarCouncilOfferBehavior defers to before it shows that inquiry. Get it wrong and the offer either never appears (no captains ever take counsel) or spams on every back-to-back battle (the anti-nag cooldown is exactly what the DefaultCooldownDays boundary pins), or surfaces to a lone player who commands no army at all._

- GIVEN in an army and a notable action never offered, WHEN evaluated, THEN it offers
- GIVEN no prior offer, WHEN evaluated, THEN the null days case is allowed
- GIVEN not in an army, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a non notable action, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a notable action within the cooldown, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a notable action after the cooldown, WHEN evaluated, THEN it offers again

### Assemblies/WardEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents sponsor_ward's eligibility gate: alive, free, within the young-adult age band, and not already the protege of another live bond (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own "un protege n'a qu'un mentor a la fois"). The STAKE across every test here: an ineligible companion (dead, imprisoned, too old, or already sponsored) taken on as a ward would either be mechanically wrong (a dead companion cannot travel with anyone) or would silently double-book a protege between two mentors, exactly the kind of drift the eligibility gate exists to prevent._

- GIVEN every condition met, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is
- GIVEN the companion is dead, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is not free, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is outside the age band, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is already sponsored, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN multiple conditions fail at once, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is still refused

### Assemblies/WardPolicyTests.cs

_Documents sponsor_ward's pure tuning: the "young companion" age band and the daily skill-XP trickle. Neither figure comes from COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md itself (unlike HostagePolicy.TermDays, which inherits Kimi's own 20-90 day range) - both are this build's own author's design call (WardPolicy's own header), so the STAKE here is narrower than most policy tests: pinning the CHOSEN values so a future tuning pass changes them deliberately, with a comment, rather than by silent drift._

- GIVEN the minimum ward age, WHEN asked, THEN it matches the vanilla adult floor
- GIVEN an age below the floor, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN an age at the floor, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is accepted
- GIVEN an age at the ceiling, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is accepted
- GIVEN an age above the ceiling, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the daily mentorship xp, WHEN asked, THEN it is a small flat trickle

## Battles

### Battles/BattleFeatDistillerTests.cs

_BattleFeatDistiller decides which of a battle's many entries an NPC would actually bring up. It is the guard against the obvious failure of this whole pillar: a battle can produce dozens of entries, and a memory that kept them all would be a casualty list rather than a recollection, bloating every profile in the save and making lords recite statistics at the player. It is equally a guard the other way: the ONE deed worth naming, and the foe who put the player down (the seed of a rivalry or a grudge), must survive the cut, or the feature records nothing worth talking about._

- GIVEN only unremarkable feats, WHEN distilled, THEN nothing is kept
- GIVEN a notable and an exploit, WHEN distilled, THEN the exploit is chosen
- GIVEN many worthy feats, WHEN distilled, THEN only one is kept
- GIVEN equal tiers, WHEN distilled, THEN the first recorded wins
- GIVEN the player was downed, WHEN distilled, THEN that foe is kept
- GIVEN an ordinary toll, WHEN distilled, THEN it is not mentioned
- GIVEN a remarkable toll, WHEN distilled, THEN it is mentioned
- GIVEN nothing of note, WHEN distilled, THEN the digest is empty

### Battles/BattleFeatTextTests.cs

_BattleFeatText writes the deeds a battle leaves in the memory of those who were there, and the version that circulates to those who were not. It guards ONE rule above all (Gabriel, ratified 2026-09-06): a LORD is bested, put down, or taken alive, and is called killed ONLY when the engine really killed them. Bannerlord knocks heroes out far more often than it kills them, so a line saying "you killed Lucon" would have NPCs mourning a man sitting in the player's own dungeon, which is exactly the amnesia-and-nonsense this whole mod exists to avoid. The second thing these pin is the VOICE: a witness speaks first-hand ("I saw you..."), the realm speaks as hearsay ("They say..."), because the recent-matter approach routes those two registers differently._

- GIVEN a bested foe, WHEN written, THEN it never claims a death
- GIVEN a foe taken alive, WHEN written, THEN the capture is stated and no death is claimed
- GIVEN a genuinely slain foe, WHEN written, THEN the death is spoken plainly
- GIVEN a witnessed deed, WHEN written, THEN it is in the first person
- GIVEN the player was downed, WHEN written, THEN the foe is named
- GIVEN an exploit that travels, WHEN written, THEN it is hearsay naming both parties
- GIVEN no engagement name, WHEN written, THEN the line has no dangling place
- GIVEN a missing foe name, WHEN written, THEN it degrades to a sayable phrase
- GIVEN the bested foe himself, WHEN written, THEN he speaks of his own defeat
- GIVEN the bested foe himself, WHEN written, THEN no death is claimed
- GIVEN the man who downed the player, WHEN written, THEN he owns the deed

### Battles/CombatProwessPolicyTests.cs

_CombatProwessPolicy decides how remarkable it is that one fighter put another down, and therefore how far word of it travels. Gabriel's ruling (2026-09-06): a world event is worth it only for an EXPLOIT, e.g. besting an opponent much stronger than yourself. Two failures matter. If the stomp rule broke, every bandit the player rides over would become realm gossip and the feature would read as spam. If the exploit bar drifted, the one deed actually worth talking about (you beat someone above your weight, or a name the realm knows) would be recorded as an ordinary scuffle and never circulate, which is the whole point of the feature._

- GIVEN two fighters alike but for level, WHEN scored, THEN the veteran rates higher
- GIVEN mobility against weapon skill, WHEN scored, THEN the weapon skill wins
- GIVEN empty or null facts, WHEN scored, THEN it is never below one
- GIVEN a foe well above the winner, WHEN rated, THEN it is an exploit that travels
- GIVEN a foe far beneath the winner, WHEN rated, THEN it is unremarkable and does not travel
- GIVEN a famous foe, WHEN rated, THEN it is an exploit even if the winner was stronger
- GIVEN an even match, WHEN rated, THEN it is notable but does not travel

## Chronicle

### Chronicle/ChronicleResumePolicyTests.cs

_that the chronicle does not lose a chapter when a deed behind it is withdrawn._

- GIVEN a chronicled deed is retracted, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it still starts after the last one it wrote
- GIVEN a retraction and a new deed, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN the new deed is the one it writes
- GIVEN nothing has been withdrawn, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it starts after the last deed
- GIVEN an older save with a count and no mark, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN the count is used as before
- GIVEN a count larger than the deeds that remain, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it is clamped to the end
- GIVEN the marked deed itself was retracted, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it falls back to the clamped count
- GIVEN a chronicle never illuminated, WHEN it resumes, THEN it starts at the beginning
- GIVEN two identical deeds on one day, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it takes the later one

## Companions

### Companions/CompanionServiceTransferPolicyTests.cs

_take_into_service, built on Gabriel's ruling "un seigneur doit pouvoir recruter" after Ira agreed in prose to take his companion Nabb under her banner and the game had nothing to execute._

- GIVEN a free lord and a free companion, WHEN evaluated, THEN the transfer is allowed
- GIVEN the companion is the players spouse, WHEN evaluated, THEN no lord may take them
- GIVEN the companion governs a fief, WHEN evaluated, THEN the fief is not quietly left empty
- GIVEN the companion is away on an errand, WHEN evaluated, THEN they must be recalled first
- GIVEN the hero is not of the players household, WHEN evaluated, THEN there is nothing to give
- GIVEN a retainer sworn in without the companion door, WHEN evaluated, THEN they can still be offered
- GIVEN the hero is the players own blood, WHEN evaluated, THEN they are not a retainer to place
- GIVEN the hero leads the players house, WHEN evaluated, THEN they are nobodys to give
- GIVEN a lord who is dead captive or the player, WHEN evaluated, THEN nobody changes hands
- GIVEN the lord is of the players own clan, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses rather than no op
- GIVEN a lord with no house of their own, WHEN evaluated, THEN there is nowhere to take them
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses rather than throwing
- GIVEN one companion and no name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it is the one standing there
- GIVEN several companions and no name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it refuses to guess
- GIVEN a short name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN the long named companion is found
- GIVEN the full name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it is matched exactly
- GIVEN two companions sharing a first name, WHEN only that name is spoken, THEN it refuses to guess
- GIVEN a full name that another shares a first word with, WHEN picking, THEN the exact one wins
- GIVEN a name nobody answers to, WHEN picking, THEN nobody is handed over

## Conversation

### Conversation/BriefingReturnDecisionTests.cs

_the SHAPE of the briefing-to-meeting sequence, which is the only thing that ever went wrong with it._

- GIVEN a map conversation and no mission, WHEN asked, THEN there is still a host
- GIVEN a mission and no map conversation, WHEN asked, THEN there is still a host
- GIVEN neither host, WHEN deciding through the same rule, THEN the world comes back
- GIVEN either host alive, WHEN deciding, THEN the meeting is opened over it
- GIVEN the chat is down and a host is live, WHEN deciding, THEN it opens over that host
- GIVEN no host at all, WHEN deciding, THEN it gives the world back rather than waiting
- GIVEN every possible situation, WHEN deciding, THEN it never asks for a scene to be built
- GIVEN the window is still closing, WHEN deciding, THEN it waits
- GIVEN the wait has run long, WHEN deciding, THEN it stops waiting and settles up
- GIVEN nothing armed, WHEN deciding, THEN it does nothing whatever else is true
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN planning the close, THEN it gives none of the world back
- GIVEN an ordinary close, WHEN planning, THEN the world comes back in order
- GIVEN either close, WHEN planning, THEN what happened is always recorded and the window always goes
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN it is not caught, THEN exactly what it skipped is owed
- GIVEN every step that exists, WHEN planning, THEN the ordinary close runs all of them
- GIVEN either plan, WHEN read, THEN no step appears twice
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN compared to an ordinary close, THEN it only ever does less

### Conversation/ConversationCastTests.cs

_Spotlight pillar foundation. A conversation starts focused on one character (the anchor) with others watching from the edge, and the player report this pillar answers is a witness who was spoken to yet stayed a mute bystander. The cast is the source of truth for who is a full participant and who is not, so the whole feature rests on these transitions being exact: a witness spoken to becomes active AND STAYS active; one told to hold their tongue drops back; and the anchor, the reason the conversation exists, can never be silenced. Get a sign wrong and either a drawn-in character keeps being ignored (the very bug) or the person you came to talk to gets shoved out of their own scene._

- GIVEN a new conversation, WHEN it opens, THEN only the anchor is active and the rest are witnesses
- GIVEN a witness, WHEN promoted, THEN they become active
- GIVEN one promoted witness, WHEN checking, THEN the scene is an ensemble
- GIVEN an already active participant, WHEN promoted again, THEN it is a no op
- GIVEN the anchor or an absent character, WHEN promoted, THEN nothing happens
- GIVEN a promoted participant, WHEN demoted, THEN they return to witness
- GIVEN the anchor, WHEN demotion is attempted, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a witness or absent character, WHEN demoted, THEN it is a no op
- GIVEN a newcomer, WHEN introduced, THEN they join as a witness and can be promoted

### Conversation/ConversationResumePolicyTests.cs

_WHEN a conversation the player walked away from may be picked up again, and when putting it back would be a lie._

- GIVEN the same lord on the same day with words still kept, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN the exchange resumes
- GIVEN the player opens a chat with somebody else, WHEN it is checked, THEN the other lords words are not put in his mouth
- GIVEN a day has passed in the campaign, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN yesterdays words are not recited as if still standing there
- GIVEN a save from an earlier day is loaded, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN it is refused like any other day that is not this one
- GIVEN nothing worth resuming, WHEN a chat opens, THEN the chat starts clean
- GIVEN the chat that opens is a staged scene rather than an ordinary talk, WHEN it is checked, THEN nothing is resumed into it
- GIVEN several reasons to refuse at once, WHEN it is checked, THEN the answer names the most fundamental one

### Conversation/ConversationResumeSnapshotTests.cs

_that the words of a conversation the player walked away from can be put back exactly as they were, and that a snapshot which cannot be read never costs anyone their save._

- GIVEN the negotiation fkasad walked away from, WHEN it is captured and restored, THEN every word comes back in order
- GIVEN a conversation already folded into a recap, WHEN it is restored, THEN the recap comes back with it
- GIVEN a conversation longer than the cap, WHEN it is captured, THEN it is the most recent words that are kept
- GIVEN folded lines are dropped by the cap, WHEN it is restored, THEN the fold marker still points at the right line
- GIVEN a captured conversation, WHEN it is written to the save and read back, THEN it still knows whose it is
- GIVEN an unreadable capture, WHEN it is read, THEN nothing is resumed and nothing throws
- GIVEN a chat that was opened and closed without a word, WHEN it is captured, THEN there is nothing to keep
- GIVEN a capture with no hero named, WHEN it is taken, THEN it is refused rather than kept for anyone
- GIVEN a scene with a witness and narration, WHEN it is restored, THEN the room comes back whole

### Conversation/EnsembleFloorPolicyTests.cs

_The case these were written from is real and is reproduced verbatim below: Gabriel briefed Nabb to charm Ira, Nabb ended his beat by turning to her and asking "Which ones do you remember?", and the floor came back to the player with the question hanging. He had to spend a turn typing "*I watch and wait to see what happens*" to make Ira answer._

- GIVEN the beat Gabriel played, WHEN it closes, THEN Ira is given the floor to answer
- GIVEN a question followed by closing narration, WHEN read, THEN it still counts as asked
- GIVEN a rhetorical question answered by the same speaker, WHEN read, THEN nothing was asked
- GIVEN a beat that ends on a statement, WHEN deciding, THEN the floor returns to the player
- GIVEN only one voice in the room, WHEN it asks, THEN the question is the players to answer
- GIVEN the asker is in the cast, WHEN choosing, THEN they are never chosen to answer themselves
- GIVEN this beat has already answered once, WHEN asked again, THEN the floor goes back to the player
- GIVEN the cap, WHEN read, THEN one beat can never spend more than a single answer
- GIVEN the last line is narration that asks, WHEN deciding, THEN no one is made to answer it
- GIVEN three voices and a name spoken, WHEN choosing, THEN the one named answers
- GIVEN three voices and no name spoken, WHEN choosing, THEN the anchor answers
- GIVEN two names in one line, WHEN choosing, THEN the one turned to last answers
- GIVEN nothing or junk, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines quietly rather than throwing
- GIVEN the player asked the last question, WHEN deciding, THEN no extra turn is spent on it

### Conversation/SilenceOrderPolicyTests.cs

_Spotlight pillar. A promoted participant can be ordered back to silence, and whether that lands is what keeps the scene believable: a lord's own vassal falling quiet on command reads true, and so does a proud rival refusing to be shushed by an equal. Gabriel's ruling is that STANDING (rank and clan influence) dominates deterministically, and temper only decides between near-equals. These pin exactly that: the liege and the wide-gap cases ignore temper, the extremes never depend on a die of personality, and inside the band an agreeable heart yields while a bold, proud one holds the floor. If standing did not dominate, an influential lord's command would be at the mercy of the target's mood, which is the opposite of what power means here._

- GIVEN the commander is the targets liege, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they obey whatever their temper
- GIVEN the commander far outweighs the target, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they obey whatever their temper
- GIVEN the commander is far beneath the target, WHEN ordering silence, THEN it is defied whatever their temper
- GIVEN a defiant target, WHEN the standing gap is wide, THEN influence wins and they comply
- GIVEN equal standing and a neutral temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they hold the floor
- GIVEN equal standing and a deferential temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they yield
- GIVEN equal standing and a proud defiant temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they refuse
- GIVEN no context, WHEN resolving, THEN the voice is kept

## Diagnostics

### Diagnostics/LogRotationPolicyTests.cs

_The log file is trimmed at every launch to the last couple of sessions so it never balloons (player request)._

- GIVEN more sessions than the budget, WHEN trimming, THEN only the most recent blocks remain
- GIVEN a budget of two, WHEN trimming, THEN the last two blocks remain in order
- GIVEN preamble before the first marker, WHEN trimming, THEN it is dropped with the old blocks
- GIVEN fewer sessions than the budget, WHEN trimming, THEN the content is returned unchanged
- GIVEN no marker in the content, WHEN trimming, THEN it is returned unchanged
- GIVEN a zero budget, WHEN trimming, THEN nothing is kept
- GIVEN null content, WHEN trimming, THEN the result is empty

## Duels

### Duels/CourtshipDuelPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: a courtship duel's romance-axis consequence. The through-line is that fighting for her (win or lose) is what a suitor is FOR, while refusing to fight for her at all is judged worse than losing._

- GIVEN the player won the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction rises by admiration on win
- GIVEN the player lost the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction falls by doubt on loss
- GIVEN the player refused the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction falls by cowardice on refuse
- GIVEN the rival balked, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction rises by admiration on rival balked
- GIVEN an unrecognized resolution, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction does not move
- GIVEN the four resolutions, WHEN compared, THEN winning beats losing beats refusing

### Duels/CourtshipRivalryEligibilityTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: is there a real triangle worth a duel? All three facts (a real courtship, a real rival desire, and a rival bold enough to force it) must hold at once. Without the triangle actually existing, a duel over a woman nobody is courting is nonsense; without rival boldness, a timid rival would never force it to steel in the first place._

- GIVEN all three facts hold, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should challenge
- GIVEN the player is not courting her, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge
- GIVEN the rival does not desire her, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge
- GIVEN the rival is not bold enough, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge

### Duels/DuelAcceptancePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: once a challenge is admissible, does the NPC actually take the field? This is where the feature lives or dies as characterisation. Ratified 2026-08-26 (player report: challenges fizzled): a duel of HONOUR is ANSWERED by default, the more so for an honourable NPC, so refusal now takes a real reason: fear turned to a calculated exit, a timid dishonourable nature, or a challenger far beneath the NPC's station (a nobody who calls out a king is dismissed, not fought). If the default drifted back to "refuse", challenges would fizzle and the feature would feel dead; if a great station gulf could not override honour, a peasant could force a king onto the field. These tests pin the named forces and, at the seams, the exact score so the curve cannot silently drift under a later tweak._

- GIVEN a peer duel of honour, WHEN deciding, THEN the default is to accept
- GIVEN a challenger far beneath the npcs station, WHEN deciding, THEN even an honourable lord refuses
- GIVEN a widening station gap, WHEN scoring, THEN it only leans the npc away from the field
- GIVEN a brave honourable lord challenged before witnesses, WHEN deciding, THEN he accepts
- GIVEN a live grudge drives it, WHEN deciding, THEN even a cautious lord takes the field
- GIVEN a calculating coward who fears the player, WHEN deciding privately, THEN he refuses
- GIVEN a calculating lord with little fear, WHEN deciding, THEN the slip rule does not bite until the threshold
- GIVEN the same honourable lord, WHEN challenged publicly versus privately, THEN the public score is higher
- GIVEN a dishonourable lord, WHEN challenged publicly, THEN the crowd pushes him further from the field
- GIVEN no context, WHEN scoring, THEN it is zero and refuses

### Duels/DuelChallengePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: the gate that decides whether a duel may even be OFFERED, before willingness is weighed. Its verdict is the bridge's law made pure: the LLM can have an NPC say "let us settle this with steel", but only an able peer, face to face, not bound by captivity or kinship or a recent bout, may be called out. Every wrong "yes" here is a duel the game cannot honour, a chat calling a commoner or a prisoner or someone across the map to a field that will never exist, and the whole feature reads as broken._

- GIVEN an able present peer, WHEN evaluating, THEN the challenge is eligible
- GIVEN a party who cannot fight, WHEN evaluating, THEN incapable outranks every other reason
- GIVEN no context at all, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is treated as incapable
- GIVEN the target is self or family, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused as kin
- GIVEN one party holds the other prisoner, WHEN evaluating, THEN captivity blocks it
- GIVEN the target is a commoner, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused as not a peer
- GIVEN the two are not in the same place, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is not face to face
- GIVEN they dueled within the cooldown, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is too soon
- GIVEN the cooldown has just elapsed, WHEN evaluating, THEN a rematch is allowed
- GIVEN they have never dueled, WHEN checking the cooldown, THEN it is not blocked

### Duels/DuelGrudgeApplicationTests.cs

_that a duel fought over a grievance actually DOES something to the grievance._

- GIVEN a duel fought over a grievance, WHEN its verdict is applied, THEN the grievance is resolved
- GIVEN a duel ducked over a grievance, WHEN its verdict is applied, THEN the grievance deepens
- GIVEN how a grudge duel ended, WHEN applied, THEN fought resolves and ducked deepens
- GIVEN a vendetta, WHEN a duel verdict is applied, THEN it is untouched
- GIVEN no grudge in play, WHEN applied, THEN nothing happens

### Duels/DuelInterlocutorPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, bugfix (player report, group conversation): a duel challenge came from a witness present in the room, "speaking on their own turn", and got mechanically armed as that witness against the player, even though the fiction described a quarrel between two OTHER people. challenge_duel carries no target parameter, so ResolveDuelChallenge always used whoever spoke as "opponent". This policy is the guard that stops that: it feeds ChatViewModel.ResolveDuelChallenge, deciding whether the speaker who just emitted the action is a fair player-vs-NPC target at all._

- GIVEN the main speaker, WHEN checking, THEN they are a direct interlocutor
- GIVEN a witness the player has addressed, WHEN checking, THEN they are a direct interlocutor
- GIVEN a witness who is not the addressed speaker, WHEN checking, THEN they are not an interlocutor
- GIVEN nobody is currently addressed, WHEN checking a witness, THEN they are not an interlocutor

### Duels/DuelOutcomePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: what a duel LEAVES BEHIND, across the four ways it can end. The through-line the whole pillar teaches is that steel honestly crossed SETTLES (respect is earned, a driving grudge is answered whoever wins) while walking away HARDENS (contempt, a grudge that festers because it was never faced). The signs are the entire lesson: if winning a duel cost the NPC's respect, or fleeing one settled a grudge, the feature would train players to do the opposite of what a duel means. Non-lethal is assumed throughout, so these are movements of regard, renown and reputation, never a death._

- GIVEN the player won a fair duel, WHEN resolving, THEN the npc respects and fears the player more and renown rises
- GIVEN the player won, WHEN more witnessed it, THEN the renown gain is larger
- GIVEN the npc won a fair duel, WHEN resolving, THEN the player still earns some respect and the npc fears them less
- GIVEN the npc won, WHEN more witnessed it, THEN the player renown does not scale with the crowd
- GIVEN a grudge drove the duel, WHEN it was fought to a finish, THEN it settles whoever won
- GIVEN a player win, WHEN it settled a grudge versus not, THEN affection ends up higher with the grudge settled
- GIVEN the player forfeited, WHEN resolving, THEN respect fear and renown all fall
- GIVEN a grudge drove it, WHEN the player forfeited, THEN the grudge hardens
- GIVEN the npc forfeited, WHEN resolving, THEN the player gains renown and a driving grudge hardens
- GIVEN an honour only stake, WHEN the player won, THEN no wager changes hands
- GIVEN a material stake, WHEN a side won the fight, THEN that side collects
- GIVEN a material stake, WHEN a side forfeited, THEN no wager changes hands
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN a side lost, THEN the loser forfeits influence
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN more witnessed the defeat, THEN the influence loss is larger
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN the crowd is enormous, THEN the influence loss is capped
- GIVEN a forfeit, WHEN resolving, THEN no influence is lost
- GIVEN no context, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing moves
- GIVEN a friendly bout the player wins, WHEN resolved, THEN it costs no fondness
- GIVEN a friendly bout the player wins, WHEN resolved, THEN the loser house is not shamed
- GIVEN a friendly bout the npc wins, WHEN resolved, THEN it still costs no fondness
- GIVEN a friendly bout nobody goes through with, WHEN resolved, THEN nothing happens
- GIVEN a duel over an affront, WHEN resolved, THEN the beaten pride still costs fondness
- GIVEN no reason supplied, WHEN a context is built, THEN it is an affront

### Duels/DuelReasonTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: DuelReason is a bare enum, but its two values must exist and stay distinct, since the host switches consequence policy (DuelOutcomePolicy alone vs DuelOutcomePolicy plus CourtshipDuelPolicy) on which one is carried. A silently merged or renamed value would misroute a courtship duel's consequence onto the wrong hero, or an honour duel into moving a romance no one is pursuing._

- GIVEN the two duel reasons, WHEN compared, THEN they are distinct

### Duels/DuelStagingSupportTests.cs

_Duels pillar: this policy decides whether the VENUE DuelVenuePolicy already chose can actually be OPENED by the game right now. It feeds DuelEligibilityResolver.CanDuelNow (the one shared gate the prompt-time teaching and the commit-time bridge both consult): a true here means challenge_duel is safe to teach or to honour; a false means the game has no mission for it yet. Both real venues are built (Wilderness through DuelMissionLauncher, TownGates through TownDuelBehavior's town-centre staging); get either wrong and a duel path that works stops being taught, or one that does not work gets promised, which is the empty-promise bug this codebase keeps closing._

- GIVEN the venue is wilderness, WHEN checking staging, THEN it can be staged
- GIVEN the venue is town gates, WHEN checking staging, THEN it can be staged
- GIVEN the venue is not now, WHEN checking staging, THEN it cannot be staged
- GIVEN the player is inside a town with a centre, WHEN asked, THEN a town duel can be held
- GIVEN the player is at a town but not inside it, WHEN asked, THEN no town duel can be held
- GIVEN a settlement with no duelling ground, WHEN asked, THEN no town duel can be held
- GIVEN the player inside a castle whose courtyard is its centre, WHEN asked, THEN the duel can be held there
- GIVEN a village whose centre is not the one a duel opens, WHEN asked, THEN no duel can be held there
- GIVEN a town duel is refused, WHEN the open field is chosen instead, THEN it can still be staged

### Duels/DuelStakesPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: what may be WAGERED on a duel, and what walking away from one costs before a crowd._

- GIVEN an honour only stake, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is always legal
- GIVEN gold the challenger can cover, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN gold beyond the challenger purse, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a non positive gold amount, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a prisoner freedom stake, WHEN the challenger holds one, THEN it is legal else refused
- GIVEN more witnesses to a refusal, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN the renown hit grows
- GIVEN a vast crowd, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a negative witness count, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN it floors to the private baseline

### Duels/DuelVenuePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: where a duel is staged, or that it cannot be staged now. At sea there is simply no field to stand on, so that branch must win over every other fact, even a (nonsensical) claim of also being in a settlement: guarding the precedence stops a future bug elsewhere from smuggling a duel onto open water._

- GIVEN the player is in a settlement and not at sea, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is town gates
- GIVEN the player is on the open land, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is wilderness
- GIVEN the player is at sea, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is not now
- GIVEN the player is somehow at sea and in a settlement, WHEN choosing, THEN sea wins and the venue is not now
- GIVEN a settlement with nowhere to fight, WHEN choosing, THEN the duel goes to the open field
- GIVEN a settlement that cannot host one, WHEN choosing, THEN the duel is not silently dropped

## Equipment

### Equipment/EquipmentLoadoutPolicyTests.cs

_EquipmentLoadoutPolicy decides whether the notable-gear clause reads a hero's civilian or battle loadout, from the scene. It exists because a player reported their finest battle gear being remarked on in a tavern/bedchamber scene where they had narrated being out of armour. Get this wrong and either the fix does nothing (a settlement scene still reads battle plate) or it over-corrects (a battlefield reads a court gown). These tests pin the exact settlement set that flips to civilian, and that every field/hostile/unknown scene stays on battle._

- GIVEN an in settlement scene, WHEN choosing the loadout, THEN it prefers civilian
- GIVEN a field or hostile or unknown scene, WHEN choosing the loadout, THEN it prefers battle

### Equipment/EquipmentModifierQualityPolicyTests.cs

_EquipmentModifierQualityPolicy is the ONE place a raw ItemModifier's facts turn into a GearQuality band, so its correctness decides whether a companion's masterwork blade or a rusty relic ever gets voiced at all (EquipmentNotabilityPolicy stays silent on anything that reads Common). The native-quality-name path must win for every vanilla modifier (it is the game's own authoritative band); the price-multiplier fallback exists only for a modded ItemModifier with no legible quality name, and its thresholds are pinned to the REAL vanilla price_factor values found in the decompiled Modules/Native/ModuleData/item_modifiers.xml (legendary=1.8, masterwork=1.5, fine=1.2, inferior=0.6, poor=0.3), so Gabriel can check them against that file directly._

- GIVEN native quality legendary, WHEN mapping, THEN it is legendary regardless of price
- GIVEN native quality masterwork or fine, WHEN mapping, THEN each reads its own band
- GIVEN native quality poor or inferior, WHEN mapping, THEN both fold into poor
- GIVEN native quality common, WHEN mapping, THEN it is common
- GIVEN no recognizable name, WHEN mapping by price, THEN each verified vanilla threshold lands correctly
- GIVEN no recognizable name, WHEN price is unmodified, THEN it is common
- GIVEN unrecognized name with a known keyword, WHEN price is inconclusive, THEN the keyword wins
- GIVEN null or blank name, WHEN mapping, THEN it falls through to price without throwing

### Equipment/EquipmentNotabilityPolicyTests.cs

_EquipmentNotabilityPolicy decides which equipped pieces are worth telling the model about and phrases them._

- GIVEN only common pieces, WHEN composing, THEN nothing is voiced
- GIVEN a masterwork sword, WHEN composing subject male, THEN he bears it
- GIVEN a legendary weapon, WHEN composing self, THEN you bear it
- GIVEN poor armor, WHEN composing subject female, THEN she wears shabby armor
- GIVEN a fine gown, WHEN composing subject female, THEN the article is present
- GIVEN an unmarked but very costly weapon, WHEN composing, THEN it is voiced as fine
- GIVEN an unmarked weapon below the value threshold, WHEN composing, THEN nothing is voiced
- GIVEN several armor pieces, WHEN composing, THEN only the best single armor clause appears
- GIVEN notable armor and weapon, WHEN composing, THEN armor leads then weapon joined by and
- GIVEN three notable kinds, WHEN composing, THEN the two most notable survive the cap
- GIVEN null or empty pieces, WHEN composing, THEN it is empty and does not throw
- GIVEN ordinary armor and weapon, WHEN composing worn, THEN it states the plain gear
- GIVEN a mix of ordinary and notable, WHEN composing worn, THEN only the notable piece is adjectived
- GIVEN armor weapon and mount, WHEN composing worn, THEN all three read in order
- GIVEN gear, WHEN composing worn in self voice, THEN it is second person
- GIVEN two weapons, WHEN composing worn, THEN the more valuable one is named
- GIVEN null or empty pieces, WHEN composing worn, THEN it is empty and does not throw

## Escort

### Escort/EscortContinuationPolicyTests.cs

_FollowMeBehavior.Advance asks EscortContinuationPolicy every daily tick whether an escort is over._

- GIVEN a quiet day with time left, WHEN judged, THEN the escort continues
- GIVEN the muster reaches him mid escort, WHEN judged, THEN he leaves for his lieges call
- GIVEN any way an escort can end, WHEN described, THEN the player is told something
- GIVEN the term has run out, WHEN judged, THEN the escort ends as agreed
- GIVEN the lord is lost to us, WHEN judged, THEN the escort ends whatever the term says
- GIVEN several endings at once, WHEN judged, THEN the gravest is the one reported
- GIVEN nothing can be read about him, WHEN judged, THEN the escort ends rather than continuing
- GIVEN no name to use, WHEN described, THEN the sentence still reads

### Escort/EscortEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_follow_me's start gate: EncounterContextBuilder.Build and FollowMeBehavior.TryStart both ask EscortEligibilityPolicy the same question from the same facts, so the prompt teaching and the bridge's own re-check can never quietly diverge ("the prompt is advice, the bridge is law")._

- GIVEN a lord with nothing in his way, WHEN asked, THEN he will ride with the player
- GIVEN his realm is at war but he is idle, WHEN asked, THEN he may still ride
- GIVEN his realm is at war, WHEN he agrees, THEN he warns the player he may be called away
- GIVEN he is committed to a war today, WHEN asked, THEN he will not promise his men
- GIVEN he leads no company of his own, WHEN asked, THEN there is nothing to escort with
- GIVEN their two realms are at war, WHEN asked, THEN he will not ride beside that army
- GIVEN he is already riding escort, WHEN asked, THEN he will not promise it twice
- GIVEN several reasons at once, WHEN asked, THEN he names the one that actually settles it
- GIVEN each reason he declines, WHEN told to the player, THEN each reads differently
- GIVEN no name to use, WHEN told to the player, THEN the sentence still reads
- GIVEN nothing could be read about him, WHEN asked, THEN no promise is made
- GIVEN an escort is agreed, WHEN asked how long, THEN it is the one fixed term

### Escort/EscortRecordTests.cs

_The mod's FollowMeBehavior persists a List<EscortRecord> as byte[] JSON via Newtonsoft.Json, mirroring CouncilStipendBehavior's own SyncData shape exactly (idempotent load, graceful on missing/corrupt). The mod project (CampaignBehaviorBase, IDataStore, Hero) is not referenced by this test project, so the behavior's actual SyncData call is integration-only (see project_testing_architecture: mod = engine, untestable here)._

- GIVEN an active escort, WHEN round tripped through the same serializer the behavior uses, THEN every field survives
- GIVEN no active escorts, WHEN round tripped, THEN it is an empty list not null
- GIVEN corrupt json, WHEN deserialized, THEN it throws so the behaviors own catch path can fall back

## Extension

### Extension/ConversationLineSequencerTests.cs

_Modder request (Heroes Voices): a TTS/subtitle addon groups CrConversation.LineSpoken events by SessionId and orders them by LineIndex, including the multi-participant SPOTLIGHT ensemble conversations 2.3.1 introduced. ConversationLineSequencer is the pure logic under test here: it hands out the monotonic LineIndex and tracks which SessionId is current. Get the reset wrong and a second conversation's lines would collide with (or continue past) the first's, so the addon groups or orders lines incorrectly and plays the wrong audio against the wrong line._

- GIVEN a started session, WHEN NextIndex is called repeatedly, THEN it yields 0 1 2
- GIVEN a session already advanced, WHEN a new session starts, THEN the counter resets to 0
- GIVEN two sessions started in sequence, WHEN reading SessionId, THEN it reflects the last StartSession call
- GIVEN a fresh sequencer, WHEN no StartSession has been called, THEN SessionId is null

## General

### ActionConfirmationControllerTests.cs

_Covers the decision logic the in-chat action-confirmation controller composes. ActionConfirmationController itself lives in the net472 mod project (engine-bound: Hero, NpcMemoryCampaignBehavior, StoryLog, ModLog, the ExecuteAction bridge), which this net10 test project cannot reference, so the controller's queue/apply flow is exercised in game rather than here (the same constraint the round-table extraction hit, see RoundTableControllerTests). What is pinned below is the pure ActionConfirmationPolicy the controller drives its every branch through: which action types get queued vs run inline (Preprocess), how a typed answer routes (HandleInput), and the exact prompt text (PromptNext / the Y/n line). Behaviour must stay identical after the extraction, so these guard the seam the controller now depends on._

- GIVEN a player outgoing spend, WHEN preprocessed, THEN it is gated for confirmation
- GIVEN an npc gift or a non coin consequence, WHEN preprocessed, THEN it runs inline
- GIVEN an empty or affirmative answer, WHEN handled, THEN the action is confirmed
- GIVEN a negative answer, WHEN handled, THEN the action is declined
- GIVEN a free text answer, WHEN handled, THEN it recomposes into a fresh turn
- GIVEN a queued take gold, WHEN prompting, THEN the question asks the player to pay
- GIVEN a queued join party, WHEN prompting, THEN the question asks about taking service

### ActionInterpreterTimingPolicyTests.cs

_Documents how the "Actions Interpreter Timing" MCM dropdown string maps to the ActionInterpreterTiming enum._

- GIVEN the synchronous label, WHEN parsing, THEN synchronous is returned
- GIVEN the asynchronous label, WHEN parsing, THEN asynchronous is returned
- GIVEN a padded or recased asynchronous value, WHEN parsing, THEN asynchronous is still returned
- GIVEN a blank null or unknown value, WHEN parsing, THEN it degrades to synchronous

### ActionMemoryLabelTests.cs

_ActionMemoryLabel turns an executed bridge action into the short blue tag the Memories panel shows beside a journal entry. The tag exists so a memory that carried a REAL mechanical consequence (gold moved, a recruit, a fief, a regard shift) reads at a glance, distinct from the LLM's soft narrative classification. What breaks if this is wrong: a deed gets no tag (the turning point is invisible), a non-deed flow control earns a spurious tag (noise), or a relation shift shows the wrong direction (the player misreads whether they gained or lost standing). These tests pin the mapping the panel depends on._

- (not stated as a rule) Gives a plain label for a coin deed
- (not stated as a rule) Colours a relation shift by direction
- (not stated as a rule) Names the service and prisoner turning points
- (not stated as a rule) Returns null for non deed flow controls
- (not stated as a rule) Returns null for a blank type
- (not stated as a rule) Humanizes an unmapped verb
- (not stated as a rule) Is case and whitespace insensitive

### ActionRefusalNoteTests.cs

_When the bridge refuses an action the prompt let through, the note this builds is what tells the MODEL._

- GIVEN a refused action, WHEN building the note, THEN it carries the reason and denies the deed
- GIVEN an action that was not refused, WHEN building the note, THEN nothing is written
- GIVEN a refusal the bridge did not explain, WHEN building the note, THEN it still denies the deed cleanly

### ActiveConflictsFormatterTests.cs

_that a kingdom at war with four neighbours is named once, not four times._

- GIVEN one kingdom at war with three, WHEN described, THEN it is named once
- GIVEN several sides, WHEN described, THEN each opens its own entry
- GIVEN a side that recurs after another, WHEN described, THEN the order is left alone
- GIVEN a single war, WHEN described, THEN it reads as it always did
- GIVEN no wars, WHEN described, THEN the line is empty
- GIVEN an unnamed side, WHEN described, THEN that war is skipped

### AddressedSpeakerDetectorTests.cs

_Regression coverage for the witness-impersonation bug: a bare MENTION of a co-located witness was being treated as a direct ADDRESS, routing the whole turn (and the LLM's voice) to them while the chat window still showed the main NPC's name. These tests pin the tightened vocative/stage-direction rules that keep a bare mention ABOUT a witness from stealing the turn, while still letting the player genuinely address a witness directly (the Sprint 15B feature)._

- GIVEN a leading vocative comma, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a comma flanked vocative mid sentence, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a vocative comma before a sentence final name, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN the name alone as the whole message, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN the name immediately followed by you, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a stage direction that turns to the witness, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a stage direction that speaks or says to the witness, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a stage direction that explicitly addresses asks or faces the witness, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a tag vocative with the comma dropped, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN another tag word before the name, WHEN detected, THEN routes to the named witness
- GIVEN a verb of mention before the name not a tag, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a question that merely ends with the name, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN the name as a bare first word report, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a stage direction that only glances at or points at the witness, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a bare stage direction naming the witness, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a name in a possessive appositive, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a kinship appositive before the name, WHEN detected, THEN does not route
- GIVEN a possessive object that is not a person before the name, WHEN detected, THEN still routes
- GIVEN a vocative address to the main npc, WHEN detected, THEN it resolves to the main npc harmlessly
- GIVEN a message with no participant names, WHEN detected, THEN no addressee is resolved so the sticky speaker is kept

### AffairDiscoveryPolicyTests.cs

_Guards AffairDiscoveryPolicy (Inc 3b-a): whether a SECRET affair is exposed to the player on a given tryst._

- GIVEN more trysts, WHEN computing the discovery chance, THEN it rises
- GIVEN no trysts yet, WHEN computing the discovery chance, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a very long affair, WHEN computing the discovery chance, THEN it clamps to the max
- GIVEN a secret unknown affair, WHEN the roll lands under the chance, THEN it is discovered
- GIVEN an open lover, WHEN asked, THEN he is never discovered
- GIVEN an already known affair, WHEN asked, THEN it is not rediscovered

### AmbushPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a town ambush springs (a roll inside the spring chance), how many companions stand with the player, and the attacking force it draws (one elite chief plus ~3 cutthroats per defender, nudged by ±1)._

- GIVEN a roll below the chance, WHEN checking, THEN the ambush springs
- GIVEN a roll at or above the chance, WHEN checking, THEN the streets stay quiet
- GIVEN no companions in the party, WHEN counting the side, THEN the player stands alone
- GIVEN companions in the party, WHEN counting the side, THEN all stand up to the cap
- GIVEN the player alone, WHEN sizing the force, THEN a chief and three cutthroats
- GIVEN a larger retinue, WHEN sizing the force, THEN the total scales with the square of the side
- GIVEN a varying roll, WHEN sizing the force, THEN the count is nudged by one either way
- GIVEN a non positive side, WHEN sizing the force, THEN it floors to a lone defender
- GIVEN a mid clan, WHEN sizing the chief, THEN he matches the player clan tier
- GIVEN a fledgling or great clan, WHEN sizing the chief, THEN it clamps to the band
- GIVEN no one has an attempt armed, WHEN entering a town, THEN nothing is decided
- GIVEN an armed assassin but the feature is off, WHEN entering a town, THEN it is disabled
- GIVEN armed and enabled, WHEN the roll misses, THEN it fizzles and re rolls next visit
- GIVEN armed and enabled, WHEN the roll lands, THEN the ambush springs

### AppearanceComposerTests.cs

_Player appearance pillar, composer increment: derived body-fact banding + authored prose, pure natural-language rendering. See AppearanceComposer's own header for the design._

- GIVEN height below the short threshold, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads short
- GIVEN height above the tall threshold, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads tall
- GIVEN average height, WHEN phrasing, THEN it is null
- GIVEN a slight build and weight, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads slightly built
- GIVEN a solid build and weight, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads solidly built
- GIVEN a powerful build and weight, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads powerfully built
- GIVEN an average build and weight, WHEN phrasing, THEN it is null
- GIVEN age just below the youth cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads youth
- GIVEN age at the youth cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads prime
- GIVEN age just below the prime cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads prime
- GIVEN age at the prime cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads middle years
- GIVEN age just below the middle cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads middle years
- GIVEN age at the middle cutoff, WHEN phrasing, THEN it reads old age
- GIVEN age zero or below, WHEN phrasing, THEN it is null
- GIVEN a tall powerful male in his middle years with dark hair, WHEN composing, THEN it reads the flagship sentence
- GIVEN a short slight female with no hair descriptor, WHEN composing, THEN the hair clause is omitted
- GIVEN unknown hair as empty or whitespace, WHEN composing, THEN the hair clause is omitted
- GIVEN a male with a beard, WHEN composing, THEN the beard clause is included
- GIVEN a skin tone, WHEN composing, THEN it reads as an adjective beside build
- GIVEN no skin tone, WHEN composing, THEN no skin adjective appears
- GIVEN only a skin tone and age, WHEN composing, THEN it still reads
- GIVEN an eye colour, WHEN composing, THEN it reads in the with clause after hair
- GIVEN hair eyes and a beard, WHEN composing, THEN the with clause joins naturally
- GIVEN hair and a beard together, WHEN composing, THEN both join in one with clause
- GIVEN a female flagged with a beard, WHEN composing, THEN the beard clause never appears
- GIVEN wholly neutral facts and empty prose, WHEN composing, THEN it is an empty string not null
- GIVEN wholly neutral facts and authored prose, WHEN composing, THEN only the authored prose is returned
- GIVEN both derived facts and authored prose, WHEN composing, THEN the authored prose is appended as its own sentence
- GIVEN a female in her prime, WHEN composing, THEN the pronoun reads her
- GIVEN a male in his prime, WHEN composing, THEN the pronoun reads his
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN composing, THEN no em dash ever appears in output
- GIVEN the self voice, WHEN composing, THEN it reads second person with a your possessive
- GIVEN a female in the self voice, WHEN composing, THEN the age possessive reads your not her
- GIVEN no explicit voice, WHEN composing, THEN it defaults to the third person subject voice

### AppreciationTonePolicyTests.cs

_This gate decides whether an intimacy-tagged scene leaves a fondly-remembered KindnessInScene appreciation, which is what the NPC's encyclopedia surfaces under "Kindnesses". It exists because of a real player report (Nexus): after a VIOLENT encounter that set the relation to -20, the encyclopedia still read "carries a small kindness remembered towards you for a tender moment we shared" because the mod planted the kindness on any turn the model tagged as intimacy, with no tone check. If this gate is wrong, either a hostile/coerced scene stamps a fake tenderness on the encyclopedia (the reported bug), or a genuinely tender moment leaves no mark at all (the feature failing to deliver). Every test pins one side of that line._

- GIVEN the same turn lowered the relation, WHEN deciding, THEN no kindness is left
- GIVEN a captive or coerced scene, WHEN deciding, THEN no kindness is left
- GIVEN a consensual tenderness that did not lower the relation, WHEN deciding, THEN a kindness is left
- GIVEN a coerced scene that also lowered the relation, WHEN deciding, THEN no kindness is left

### ArrangedMatchPolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides whether an arranged cross-clan match is CONSENTED TO: the player proposes marrying a member of their own clan to a noble of another clan, and the target family either agrees or refuses. It is consumed by the mod's arrange_marriage bridge action, which applies the real marriage only when ArrangedMatchPolicy.Decide returns Agreed AND the engine's own MarriageModel.IsCoupleSuitableForMarriage also passes. If this drifts, either a family marries its kin away for nothing (RefusedStanding wrongly returning Agreed), or the feature never fires at all (Agreed wrongly returning RefusedStanding). It is deliberately systems-driven, not an LLM judgment call, after a player report (CarolusIV, Nexus, 2026-07-25) that a weak local model "gets confused a lot" and must not be talkable into an unwarranted match._

- GIVEN the target is of the players own clan, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not cross clan
- GIVEN regard at or above the threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN the family agrees
- GIVEN low regard but the players clan outranks the target, WHEN deciding, THEN the family agrees
- GIVEN low regard and no tier advantage, WHEN deciding, THEN the family refuses
- GIVEN regard exactly at the threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN the family agrees
- GIVEN regard one below the threshold and no tier advantage, WHEN deciding, THEN the family refuses
- GIVEN equal clan tiers and low regard, WHEN deciding, THEN the family refuses

### AssemblyHostValidityTests.cs

_Pins the host-validity rule: whether a convened assembly's chosen town or castle is still somewhere the gathering can be held. The behavior re-checks this each day and on siege / ownership events; a non-Valid verdict CANCELS the assembly._

- GIVEN a standing peaceful host, WHEN evaluated, THEN it is valid
- GIVEN a host that no longer exists, WHEN evaluated, THEN it is Gone
- GIVEN a besieged host, WHEN evaluated, THEN it is Besieged
- GIVEN a host now held by an enemy, WHEN evaluated, THEN it is EnemyHeld

### AssemblyTravelOutcomeTests.cs

_Pins what becomes of an invitee who set out in person when the world moves under them: the behavior re-checks every EnRoute member and applies this verdict (keep coming / send an ambassador / the seat falls empty)._

- GIVEN a hero still on track, WHEN evaluated, THEN they keep arriving
- GIVEN a hero killed on the road, WHEN evaluated, THEN the seat falls empty
- GIVEN a hero taken prisoner, WHEN evaluated, THEN an ambassador stands in
- GIVEN a hero pinned in battle or siege, WHEN evaluated, THEN an ambassador stands in
- GIVEN a hero who cannot reach in time, WHEN evaluated, THEN an ambassador stands in

### AuthoredRomanticPreferenceTests.cs

_an author's ability to write a character's romantic preferences by hand in character_overrides.json, and what happens when they mistype one._

- GIVEN authored preference names, WHEN parsed, THEN they become the real preferences
- GIVEN sloppy casing and whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it is still understood
- GIVEN a typo among good names, WHEN parsed, THEN the good ones survive and nothing throws
- GIVEN a typo, WHEN asking what went wrong, THEN the offending word is named
- GIVEN the placeholder None, WHEN authored, THEN it is ignored and reported
- GIVEN the field is omitted, WHEN read, THEN it is null where an empty list is a real lock
- GIVEN an entry with only a preference, WHEN asked, THEN it counts as authored content
- GIVEN a bad preference, WHEN listing authoring problems, THEN it is reported like the others

### AutonomousSchemePolicyTests.cs

_AutonomousSchemePolicy is the FRONT of the Living World feud chain: it decides which lords may plot against which rivals on their OWN initiative, and how often. It feeds SchemeStore's weekly launcher, whose plots then resolve through Inc 1's FeudRelationPolicy (souring real relations so vanilla can escalate to war on its own)._

- GIVEN room under the cap, WHEN checking the budget, THEN a launch is allowed
- GIVEN the cap is reached, WHEN checking the budget, THEN no launch is allowed
- GIVEN a lord who never plotted, WHEN checking the cooldown, THEN it has elapsed
- GIVEN a lord who recently plotted, WHEN checking the cooldown, THEN it holds until the window elapses
- GIVEN a free living lord of another house, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they may instigate
- GIVEN the player, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they are never an autonomous instigator
- GIVEN a member of the players clan, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they are never an autonomous instigator
- GIVEN a prisoner or a non lord, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they may not instigate
- GIVEN a bitter rival who is no ones kin, WHEN checking the target, THEN they are grounded
- GIVEN a friend or a neutral lord, WHEN checking the target, THEN they are never grounded
- GIVEN close kin however hated, WHEN checking the target, THEN they are never grounded
- GIVEN the player side however hated, WHEN checking the target, THEN they are never grounded
- GIVEN a faction leader however hated, WHEN checking the target, THEN they are never grounded
- GIVEN several rivals, WHEN selecting a target, THEN the deepest enmity is chosen
- GIVEN the bitterest candidates are all excluded, WHEN selecting a target, THEN the eligible rival is chosen
- GIVEN no genuine rival, WHEN selecting a target, THEN nothing is chosen

### AwaitingReunionPolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.2 companion hardening. AwaitingReunionPolicy sizes the timeout CompanionMissionBehavior.Advance reads in its AwaitingReunion phase. Before this, that phase resolved ONLY when the companion was free AND back in the player's party; a companion who was ransomed, escaped to a wanderer state, or whose errand party dissolved never re-entered the party on their own and stayed IsAway == true FOREVER, blocking re-dispatch, expel, and their journal, with no notice. These tests pin the timeout boundary that force-closes the stranded errand._

- GIVEN a companion just entered awaiting reunion, WHEN asked to force close, THEN no
- GIVEN a wait one day short of the timeout, WHEN asked to force close, THEN no
- GIVEN a wait of exactly the timeout, WHEN asked to force close, THEN yes
- GIVEN a wait well past the timeout, WHEN asked to force close, THEN yes

### BanditClearOfferPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the "offered => completable" invariant for open-field bandit-clearing deeds. The viable-quest scanner used to offer bandit_clear whenever an anchor town existed, even when no bandit party roamed there, so a lord could send the player after bandits that were not present; the player found nothing, reported so, and the giver turned hostile (player report). ViableQuestScanner now counts bandit parties within QuestDeedTracker's own bandit-clear completion radius of the anchor and asks this policy whether to offer the deed. If this rule drifts, either the empty-hunt returns (a deed is offered with no bandits to find) or a legitimate offer is suppressed while bandits genuinely roam nearby._

- GIVEN no bandits roam nearby, WHEN deciding, THEN the deed is not offered
- GIVEN at least one bandit roams nearby, WHEN deciding, THEN the deed is offered
- GIVEN a defensive negative count, WHEN deciding, THEN the deed is not offered

### BastardBlackmailPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a bastard's mother can extort the player and how steep her price is: she has leverage only while the secret is hidden, and her demand tracks the player's wealth within a floor-and-ceiling band._

- GIVEN a hidden bastard and a living unmarried mother, WHEN checking, THEN she holds leverage
- GIVEN the secret is already out, WHEN checking, THEN there is nothing left to sell
- GIVEN a dead mother, WHEN checking, THEN she holds no leverage
- GIVEN the mother is the players own spouse, WHEN checking, THEN she holds no leverage
- GIVEN a poor player, WHEN demanding, THEN the price is floored
- GIVEN a comfortable player, WHEN demanding, THEN she takes a share of the wealth
- GIVEN a fabulously rich player, WHEN demanding, THEN the price is capped
- GIVEN the settlement cap, WHEN checking the ceiling, THEN no demand is impayable in one payment
- GIVEN a fabulously rich player, WHEN demanding, THEN the demand is settleable in one payment
- GIVEN no or negative gold, WHEN demanding, THEN it floors at the minimum
- GIVEN a payment now, WHEN setting silence, THEN it lasts the grace period
- GIVEN no outstanding demand, WHEN checking a lapse, THEN nothing lapses
- GIVEN an unmet demand within the deadline, WHEN checking, THEN she waits
- GIVEN an unmet demand past the deadline, WHEN checking, THEN she leaks
- GIVEN an active demand, WHEN checking, THEN she does not raise another
- GIVEN paid silence still in force, WHEN checking, THEN she stays quiet
- GIVEN the silence has expired and no demand stands, WHEN checking, THEN she may demand again

### BastardDiscoveryPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the discovery curve of a hidden bastard: a secret nobody knows can never leak; each extra knower raises the odds it surfaces; a crowd of knowers is capped short of certainty in any single period._

- GIVEN an infant, WHEN a season passes, THEN the risk is only the floor
- GIVEN an older child, WHEN a season passes, THEN the risk has grown with age
- GIVEN each added year, WHEN evaluating, THEN the risk never falls
- GIVEN a long kept secret, WHEN evaluating, THEN the seasonal risk is capped
- GIVEN a nonsensical negative age, WHEN evaluating, THEN it floors at the base

### BastardLegitimacyPolicyTests.cs

_Bastards (romance audit M-G2): whether a player-parented child is a concealed bastard. The bug this locks: the old test read the player's spouse LIVE at birth, so a spouse who died DURING the pregnancy (Spouse already null) turned a child conceived inside the marriage into a "concealed bastard", triggering seasonal discovery rolls and a scandal that cost the child's own legitimate line renown. The line between a posthumous marital heir and a true bastard sits on ONE fact, whether the former spouse is now dead (widowhood) or alive (divorce), so it is pinned here in both directions._

- GIVEN a child by the current spouse, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not a bastard
- GIVEN a posthumous child by a deceased former spouse, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not a bastard
- GIVEN a child by a living former spouse, WHEN deciding, THEN it is a bastard
- GIVEN a child by a never married partner, WHEN deciding, THEN it is a bastard
- GIVEN no player parentage or no other parent, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not a bastard

### BastardMotherPolicyTests.cs

_Documents that a bastard's mother is not a uniform extortionist: her traits and regard for the player decide whether she blackmails, longs for him, resents him, or simply asks for the child's keep._

- GIVEN a scheming dishonourable woman, WHEN resolving, THEN she blackmails
- GIVEN a calculating woman who is nonetheless fond, WHEN resolving, THEN fondness rules out blackmail
- GIVEN an honourable fond woman, WHEN resolving, THEN she longs for him
- GIVEN a woman who feels wronged, WHEN resolving, THEN she is resentful
- GIVEN a neutral woman, WHEN resolving, THEN she is pragmatic
- GIVEN regard at the fond and cold thresholds, WHEN resolving, THEN the tiers are inclusive
- GIVEN scheming just short of the threshold, WHEN resolving, THEN she does not blackmail

### BastardPrestigePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the prestige gate on a grown bastard's claim: a child of a grander maternal house follows that dynasty; a child of an equal-or-lesser house has motive to claim the father's clan. The flagship case is the player siring a child on Empress Rhagaea — the child wants the imperial line, not the player's clan._

- GIVEN a grander maternal house, WHEN resolving, THEN the bastard follows the mothers dynasty
- GIVEN a lesser maternal house, WHEN resolving, THEN the bastard presses a claim
- GIVEN two houses of equal standing, WHEN resolving, THEN the bastard still has motive to claim
- GIVEN a ruling dynasty, WHEN scoring, THEN it towers over an equal tier vassal
- GIVEN negative renown, WHEN scoring, THEN it never subtracts from the tier
- GIVEN the empress case, WHEN resolving, THEN her child covets nothing of a minor player clan

### BastardScandalPolicyTests.cs

_Documents that a discovered bastard cuts deeper in a strictly-monogamous culture and barely stings in a permissive one — reusing the jealousy system's per-culture outrage._

- GIVEN a strictly monogamous culture, WHEN pricing a scandal, THEN it is grave
- GIVEN a permissive culture, WHEN pricing a scandal, THEN it mostly shrugs
- GIVEN a grave culture, WHEN scaling a cost, THEN it is amplified
- GIVEN a permissive culture, WHEN scaling a cost, THEN it is softened
- GIVEN a neutral culture, WHEN scaling a cost, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN a positive base in a permissive culture, WHEN scaling, THEN it never vanishes

### BattleMemoryTextTests.cs

_Pins the "fought beside you" line and the place/engagement phrasing WorldEventFeeders writes into profiles and the chronicle. A player reported (08/08/2026) that party companions never got the memory allied lords did; the fix routes both through the single AllySummary string. A second player report (17/08/2026): a SIEGE was recorded as an "open battle near" the settlement, and a battle fought as part of an army read as a solo win. So the place clause is now setting-aware and the ally line reflects a shared effort. These pin all of that wording._

- GIVEN no player name, WHEN writing the ally line, THEN it falls back to naming them the player
- GIVEN a won solo battle, WHEN writing the ally line, THEN it credits the player
- GIVEN a lost solo battle, WHEN writing the ally line, THEN it reads as standing together
- GIVEN a named place, WHEN writing the ally line, THEN the place clause is appended cleanly
- GIVEN a shared effort win, WHEN writing the ally line, THEN it reads as a joint victory
- GIVEN a setting, WHEN building the place clause, THEN it names the engagement
- GIVEN no settlement, WHEN building the place clause, THEN it is empty
- GIVEN a setting, WHEN naming the chronicle engagement, THEN it reads as a noun phrase

### BattleNotabilityTests.cs

_Protects the rule that bounds memory growth: only sieges and two-army battles get recorded. A drift either floods the save with skirmish noise, or stops recording the battles that should be remembered._

- GIVEN a siege, WHEN judging, THEN it is notable whatever the armies
- GIVEN a clash of two armies, WHEN judging, THEN it is notable
- GIVEN only one side fields an army, WHEN judging, THEN it is not notable
- GIVEN a plain field skirmish, WHEN judging, THEN it is not notable
- GIVEN a player battle at or above the size threshold, WHEN judging, THEN it is notable without armies
- GIVEN a player battle below the size threshold, WHEN judging, THEN it is not notable
- GIVEN a player siege below the size threshold, WHEN judging, THEN it is still notable

### BirthLetterPolicyTests.cs

_Player report (2026-07-10): after marrying the mother of his child, a player kept getting child-support demand letters from her while she rode in his own party, escalating all the way to a divorce for "unpaid support". Two rulings fixed here: the player's own spouse never duns him for their shared child (kept in the household, not a courier debt), and nobody riding beside the player writes ANY birth letter at all (the game already shows the birth in person). LetterTriggerEvaluator gates both the announcement and the support request on these two booleans; get either wrong and the divorce-for-unpaid-support spiral returns._

- GIVEN the mother rides with the player, WHEN a child is born, THEN no announcement letter is sent
- GIVEN the mother is far from the player, WHEN a child is born, THEN a fond announcement is sent
- GIVEN the players own spouse gives birth apart, WHEN deciding support, THEN she never dun him
- GIVEN a child born outside marriage and the mother apart, WHEN deciding support, THEN she may ask
- GIVEN the mother rides with the player, WHEN deciding support, THEN she never writes for it

### BodyHurtPolicyTests.cs

_that all four answers about a body are actually reachable, and that each one means what it says._

- GIVEN a lord at sixty percent, WHEN asked, THEN he is knocked about rather than whole
- GIVEN the whole range of health, WHEN walked, THEN every band is reachable
- GIVEN a man in good health, WHEN read, THEN he is whole and says nothing
- GIVEN the engine calls him wounded, WHEN read, THEN the answer is never merely bruised
- GIVEN two wounded men, WHEN compared, THEN the worse hurt of them is the worse hurt
- GIVEN a body with no numbers, WHEN read, THEN nothing is claimed
- GIVEN a hardier hero, WHEN read, THEN the bands still mean the same thing

### CaptiveActionOutcomePolicyTests.cs

_Documents who decides a captive player's physical attempt, and how health governs it. The stake is the railroad Gabriel play-tested on 2026-07-20: every attempt the player typed was absorbed with no effect because the LLM judged its own scene. This policy takes that judgement away from the model, so the verdict is real, unbiased, tunable, and testable. It also carries the attrition loop: the captor's harm lowers real hit points, so these same numbers quietly strip the player's agency as the scene wears them down._

- GIVEN a player at deaths door, WHEN asked, THEN only the floor chance remains
- GIVEN a player at full health, WHEN asked, THEN the attempt is certain
- GIVEN a wounded player, WHEN asked, THEN the chance falls with the health
- GIVEN a roll on the boundary, WHEN resolved, THEN the chance means exactly what it says
- GIVEN an out of range health, WHEN asked, THEN it clamps instead of breaking

### CaptiveEncounterPolicyTests.cs

- GIVEN a capped base plus bonuses, WHEN computing probability, THEN the total is capped
- GIVEN an incompatible player, WHEN selecting intent, THEN the scene is an interrogation
- GIVEN an incompatible player and a sadistic captor, WHEN scanning every roll, THEN torture is never drawn
- GIVEN a compatible player, WHEN selecting intent, THEN the weighted draw is unchanged
- GIVEN a female prisoner and a low roll, WHEN selecting bandit intent, THEN it is personal desire
- GIVEN a few days and no kinks, WHEN computing probability, THEN it climbs with the days
- GIVEN a kink that carries no bonus, WHEN computing probability, THEN only the base applies
- GIVEN a lone fighter, WHEN capping collective participants, THEN no extra bandit can join
- GIVEN a long captivity, WHEN computing probability, THEN the base caps
- GIVEN a male prisoner, WHEN selecting bandit intent, THEN the spoil chance is near nil
- GIVEN a non desire scene, WHEN reading the collective chance, THEN it is 25
- GIVEN a personal desire scene, WHEN reading the collective chance, THEN it is 55
- GIVEN a praise kink and warm regard, WHEN computing the total weight, THEN it is larger than without the praise kink
- GIVEN a sadistic captor and neutral regard, WHEN the roll lands at the bottom of the range, THEN it is still interrogation not torture
- GIVEN a sadistic captor and neutral regard, WHEN the roll lands at the top of the range, THEN it leans torture
- GIVEN a two fighter band, WHEN capping collective participants, THEN one extra bandit can join
- GIVEN an extreme kink and regard combination, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN all four floor intents stay reachable
- GIVEN an incompatible orientation, WHEN selecting bandit intent, THEN it falls to the menace ladder
- GIVEN extra bandits and a roll at or above the chance, WHEN deciding whether the scene is collective, THEN it is not
- GIVEN extra bandits and a roll under the chance, WHEN deciding whether the scene is collective, THEN it is
- GIVEN mildly negative regard, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN reward is never drawn
- GIVEN no extra bandits, WHEN deciding whether the scene is collective, THEN it never rolls and stays solo
- GIVEN no training kink, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN training is never drawn
- GIVEN null kinks, WHEN computing probability, THEN only the base applies
- GIVEN one extra bandit, WHEN counting bandit witnesses, THEN only one is shown
- GIVEN predatory kinks, WHEN computing probability, THEN their bonuses add
- GIVEN strong contempt, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN reward is never drawn
- GIVEN the menace ladder, WHEN the second roll is 75 or more, THEN it is revenge
- GIVEN the menace ladder, WHEN the second roll is between 45 and 74, THEN it is intimidation
- GIVEN the menace ladder, WHEN the second roll is under 45, THEN it is extortion
- GIVEN the training kink, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN training is reachable
- GIVEN three extra bandits, WHEN counting bandit witnesses, THEN it is capped at two
- GIVEN warm regard, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN reward becomes reachable

### CaptiveGearLossPolicyTests.cs

_Captive-escape gear consequence: pins the ONE rule the owner is still weighing (2026-07-21), whether equipment loss ties to FLEEING or to the moment of CAPTURE. Today's ratified answer is FLED only. These three tests are the seam: when the owner decides, this file (and CaptiveGearLossPolicy alone) is what changes, not the holder ledger, the best-per-slot policy, or the recovery routes._
