- GIVEN the player fled, WHEN deciding whether gear is taken, THEN it is taken
- GIVEN the player fought free, WHEN deciding whether gear is taken, THEN it is not taken
- GIVEN the player was overpowered, WHEN deciding whether gear is taken, THEN it is not taken

### CaptiveGearStockPolicyTests.cs

_Captive-escape gear consequence: pins the "most advantageous piece per slot" rule Gabriel ratified as DETERMINISTIC (tier, then value, then a fixed ordinal key) and the additive-across-escapes accumulation._

- GIVEN two items in the same slot with different tiers, WHEN selecting worn, THEN the higher tier wins
- GIVEN two items of equal tier, WHEN selecting worn, THEN the higher value wins
- GIVEN two items tied on tier and value, WHEN selecting worn, THEN the pick is stable regardless of pool order
- GIVEN items across a weapon slot and the mount slots, WHEN selecting worn, THEN each slot resolves independently
- GIVEN an empty or null pool, WHEN selecting worn, THEN an empty result is returned without throwing
- GIVEN one item beats another in the same slot, WHEN selecting the remainder, THEN the loser is still present
- GIVEN two field identical duplicate items, WHEN resolving worn and remainder, THEN exactly one of each is kept
- GIVEN a mixed pool across several slots, WHEN summing worn and remainder, THEN every original item is accounted for
- GIVEN a holders existing stock, WHEN merging a fresh haul, THEN both hauls are present afterward
- GIVEN a null existing stock, WHEN merging a first haul, THEN the haul alone is returned without throwing
- GIVEN both lists are null, WHEN merging, THEN an empty list is returned without throwing

### CaptiveIntentRulesTests.cs

_Documents the captive-intent classification extracted from ChatViewModel: the memory verb, and which intents carry a forced-body toll / a public humiliation._

- GIVEN each intent, WHEN asking the verb, THEN it reads in first person past tense
- GIVEN a bandit menace intent, WHEN asking the verb, THEN it falls back to the default
- GIVEN forced intents, WHEN classified, THEN they carry a forced body toll
- GIVEN interrogation or reward, WHEN classified, THEN no forced toll and no humiliation
- GIVEN torture or domination, WHEN classified, THEN they are humiliating
- GIVEN reward, WHEN mapped to a grudge severity, THEN it is benign zero
- GIVEN interrogation, WHEN mapped to a grudge severity, THEN it is mild one
- GIVEN domination training or personal desire, WHEN mapped to a grudge severity, THEN they are moderate two
- GIVEN torture, WHEN mapped to a grudge severity, THEN it is grievous three
- GIVEN a bandit menace intent, WHEN mapped to a grudge severity, THEN it falls back to zero

### CaptiveKinDescriberTests.cs

_Pins the captivity clause appended to a kinsman in an NPC's RELATIONSHIPS block. Born from a player report: a parent called the player a liar for claiming to hold their child, because the relationships prompt named the captured child with no captivity marker at all. These tests fix the exact wording that closes that denial, and guard the free-kin default so ordinary family lines are never decorated._

- GIVEN a kinsman the player holds, WHEN marking, THEN the listener is named the captor
- GIVEN a kinsman held by someone else, WHEN marking, THEN it reads a plain captivity
- GIVEN a free kinsman, WHEN marking, THEN no clause is added
- GIVEN a free kinsman with a stale captor flag, WHEN marking, THEN still no clause

### CaptiveSceneArcPolicyTests.cs

_Captive scene replayability: this policy decides which named dramatic ARC (SlowBurn / Classic / Sudden) a captor's opening act follows, so the same captor does not run the identical pacing every scene. Its answer feeds CaptiveSceneDirector, the state machine that owns scene structure so the model never has to (and a hard act cap guarantees the scene actually ends, replacing the old behaviour where a scene could loop and never conclude). Every arc keeps a floor weight so none is ever unreachable; the weighted LEAN (not a hard gate) is what gives a bandit's impatience or a lord's savoured cruelty a distinct feel without removing variety. If the weighting or the fallback-on-out-of-range roll is wrong, either a captor's pacing collapses to a single repetitive arc again, or an edge-case roll could throw instead of resolving safely._

- GIVEN a bandit captor, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN every arc is still reachable
- GIVEN a bandit captor, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN sudden wins the most rolls
- GIVEN a lord after domination, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN slowburn wins the most rolls
- GIVEN a lord after training, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN slowburn wins the most rolls
- GIVEN a lord after personal desire, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN slowburn and classic are balanced
- GIVEN a lord interrogation scene, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN classic wins the most rolls
- GIVEN a lord reward or torture scene, WHEN scanning every possible roll, THEN every arc stays reachable
- GIVEN the same context and roll, WHEN selecting twice, THEN the result is deterministic
- GIVEN an out of contract roll, WHEN selecting, THEN it falls back to classic

### CaptiveSceneDirectorTests.cs

_The dramatic structure of a captive scene (Intro/RisingTension/Initiate/Intensify/Climax/Aftermath/Conclude, across one or more "acts") is owned entirely by this state machine so the LLM never has to invent pacing and never loops the same beat. It decides three things every turn: which stage plays next, who acts (the lead captor, another single witness, or the whole group at once), and when the scene is actually over. Get the sequence wrong and a scene either stalls (never reaches Conclude, the player stuck with no way out) or skips its come-down beat (Aftermath) straight to Conclude with no closure narration. The hard MaxActs cap is the last-resort guarantee every scene terminates even when every roll favours "another round" (this whole arc draw exists because the same captor used to retell the identical scene every time)._

- GIVEN a collective scene with one other, WHEN they take their turn, THEN their act is initiate then climax
- GIVEN a collective scene with two others, WHEN the dice pick together, THEN after the lead a group act opens
- GIVEN a concluding scene, WHEN advance is requested, THEN it stays concluded
- GIVEN a new scene, WHEN created, THEN the opening beat is the leads intro
- GIVEN a scene that always rolls continue, WHEN advanced relentlessly, THEN it still concludes within the act cap
- GIVEN a solo scene past the lead climax, WHEN the dice say continue, THEN a new lead act opens at initiate
- GIVEN a solo scene past the lead climax, WHEN the dice say stop, THEN the scene concludes
- GIVEN an intervention was flagged, WHEN the story advances, THEN the intervention flag clears
- (not stated as a rule) Given NoRandomSource When Constructing Then ItThrows
- GIVEN a context that draws the slowburn arc, WHEN walked, THEN rising tension precedes initiate and the tail is aftermath then conclude
- GIVEN a context that draws the sudden arc, WHEN walked, THEN it skips straight to climax and the tail is aftermath then conclude
- GIVEN a relentless bandit scene, WHEN advanced past the act cap, THEN it still reaches conclude via aftermath
- GIVEN the lead act, WHEN advanced in turn, THEN it walks intro initiate intensify climax
- GIVEN the player tries something else, WHEN developing in place, THEN the stage holds and intervention is flagged

### CaptivityReleaseNarratorTests.cs

_What the world is told when the player gets out of captivity, and whether anyone is named for it._

- GIVEN a lord broke the band holding the player, WHEN chronicling, THEN he is named
- GIVEN a liberator is known, WHEN chronicling, THEN the player is not said to have freed themself
- GIVEN a liberator is known, WHEN chronicling, THEN no motive is asserted
- GIVEN no liberator, WHEN chronicling, THEN the original wording is kept
- GIVEN the liberator could not be named, WHEN chronicling, THEN the release is still recorded
- GIVEN the captors were engaged by a known attacker, WHEN judging, THEN the attacker is credited
- GIVEN the player slipped away during the attack, WHEN chronicling, THEN both truths are told
- GIVEN the player broke loose in the din, WHEN chronicling, THEN the attacker is named without claiming the rescue
- GIVEN the same liberator, WHEN the manner differs, THEN the wording differs but the name stays
- GIVEN the winner is the captor themselves, WHEN judging, THEN no one is credited
- GIVEN the captors were not in this battle at all, WHEN judging, THEN no one is credited
- GIVEN the winning side has no leader, WHEN judging, THEN no one is credited

### CaptorReleasePolicyTests.cs

_Captivity fix (ratified 2026-07-05): decides WHETHER a captor can be brought to free the PLAYER at all, via two paths, persuasion (talked round) and ransom (gold buys freedom), so the bridge only ever teaches/executes a release the captor would actually grant ("the prompt is advice, the bridge is law"). A VENDETTA captor (a nemesis, or a grudge at/above the floor) refuses both: they did not take the player to let them buy their way out. If this policy is wrong, either a captor holding real blood-hatred can be talked or ransomed free (the vendetta becomes hollow), or an ordinary disposed captor is wrongly locked into refusing every release, leaving the player stuck with no negotiated way out at all._

- GIVEN a nemesis vendetta, WHEN resolving release, THEN the captor refuses every path
- GIVEN a heavy grudge at the vendetta floor, WHEN resolving release, THEN the captor refuses every path
- GIVEN a merciful captor with no grudge, WHEN resolving release, THEN both persuasion and ransom are open
- GIVEN a cruel captor with a moderate grudge, WHEN resolving release, THEN persuasion is closed but ransom stays open
- GIVEN a cruel captor with only a light grudge, WHEN resolving release, THEN they can still be talked round
- GIVEN a merciful captor with a moderate grudge, WHEN resolving release, THEN mercy keeps persuasion open
- GIVEN a grudge one point below the vendetta floor, WHEN resolving release, THEN ransom is still open

### CardinalDirectionPolicyTests.cs

_Phrases a bandit-hideout quest's direction hint ("the hideout lies to the north of Pravend"), consumed by QuestFactory and ViableQuestScanner as quest.DirectionHint = "{bearing} of {anchor}"._

- GIVEN a point due east, WHEN describing, THEN it reads east
- GIVEN a point due north, WHEN describing, THEN it reads north
- GIVEN a point due west, WHEN describing, THEN it reads west
- GIVEN a point due south, WHEN describing, THEN it reads south
- GIVEN a point up and to the right, WHEN describing, THEN it reads north east
- GIVEN a point down and to the left, WHEN describing, THEN it reads south west
- GIVEN two coincident points, WHEN describing, THEN it reads nearby
- GIVEN a bearing measured from an offset origin, WHEN describing, THEN it is relative to that origin

### CharacterOverrideTests.cs

_Guards player-authored character data: traits clamp to the engine band and unset ones are left alone, and resolution never hands one hero another's authored personality (id wins; an id-less homonym is refused, not guessed)._

- GIVEN out of band traits, WHEN clamping, THEN each is pinned to minus2 to plus2
- GIVEN only a backstory, WHEN checking content, THEN it counts as authored
- GIVEN only a conviction, WHEN checking content, THEN it counts as authored
- GIVEN only traits or a kink lock, WHEN checking content, THEN it counts as authored
- GIVEN an entry with no authored fields, WHEN checking content, THEN it is not authored
- GIVEN only a misspelled orientation, WHEN checking content, THEN it still counts as authored
- GIVEN a misspelled orientation, WHEN listing problems, THEN the offending word is named
- GIVEN a mistyped kink, WHEN listing problems, THEN the offending name is reported
- GIVEN a clean entry, WHEN listing problems, THEN there are none
- GIVEN unset traits, WHEN listing, THEN only the authored ones appear
- GIVEN no traits, WHEN asked, THEN HasTraits is false and the list is empty
- GIVEN blank backstory, WHEN asked, THEN HasBackstory is false
- GIVEN blank conviction, WHEN asked, THEN HasConviction is false
- GIVEN a conviction and no backstory, WHEN asked, THEN only HasConviction is true
- GIVEN kinks omitted, WHEN parsing, THEN ParsedKinks is null
- GIVEN an empty kinks list, WHEN parsing, THEN ParsedKinks is an empty non null list
- GIVEN valid kink names, WHEN parsing, THEN ParsedKinks maps them in order
- GIVEN an id match, WHEN resolving, THEN id wins even if the name differs
- GIVEN no id match, WHEN resolving, THEN a single name match is used
- GIVEN no match at all, WHEN resolving, THEN it returns null
- GIVEN two entries with the same name and no id, WHEN resolving, THEN it refuses rather than guess
- GIVEN homonyms disambiguated by id, WHEN resolving, THEN the right one wins
- GIVEN a set with entries, WHEN reading All, THEN the constructed entries are returned
- GIVEN an injected override, WHEN resolving by id, THEN the injected backstory is returned
- GIVEN an injection for a hero that already has a file entry, WHEN resolving, THEN the injection wins by id
- GIVEN an injection for one hero, WHEN resolving others, THEN the rest of the set is preserved
- GIVEN a null override, WHEN added, THEN the set is unchanged

### ChatCompositionModePolicyTests.cs

_Documents how the "Chat Composition" MCM dropdown string maps to the ChatCompositionMode enum (Phase 2): the cheap single-call STANDARD, or the full ENHANCED pipeline (PROSE + INTERPRET + NSFW fallback), which is the default._

- GIVEN the standard label, WHEN parsing, THEN standard is returned
- GIVEN the enhanced label, WHEN parsing, THEN enhanced is returned
- GIVEN a padded or recased standard value, WHEN parsing, THEN standard is still returned
- GIVEN a legacy label, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the matching new mode
- GIVEN a blank null or unknown value, WHEN parsing, THEN it degrades to enhanced

### ChatFocusArbitrationPolicyTests.cs

_when the chat overlay stands aside for something above it, and, far more importantly, when it must NOT._

- GIVEN a settlement menu that was already up, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat holds its own backdrop
- GIVEN a modal opening over the backdrop, WHEN arbitrating, THEN it still takes the screen
- GIVEN no backdrop recorded, WHEN arbitrating, THEN every focus claim is judged on its own merits
- GIVEN an unnamed claimant, WHEN a backdrop is recorded, THEN it is treated as new rather than scenery
- GIVEN a backdrop was recorded, WHEN the vanilla conversation window arrives late, THEN it is still exempt
- GIVEN vanilla conversation layer arrives after the chat, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat holds the screen
- GIVEN the map conversation window, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat still holds the screen
- GIVEN a real modal opens over the chat, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat stands aside
- GIVEN a modal beside the exempt conversation layer, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the modal still wins
- GIVEN another screen was pushed over the host, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat stands aside
- GIVEN a modal that is being torn down, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat takes the screen back
- GIVEN the world beneath the chat, WHEN arbitrating, THEN it is never read as a screen above
- GIVEN a modal over the world, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the modal is still honoured
- GIVEN only the chat itself claims focus, WHEN arbitrating, THEN it is not its own reason to yield
- GIVEN the ordinary mission hud, WHEN arbitrating, THEN none of it counts as a screen above
- GIVEN nothing readable on the host, WHEN arbitrating, THEN the chat holds rather than disappears
- GIVEN an unnamed layer claims focus, WHEN arbitrating, THEN it is honoured and still reported
- GIVEN the exemption list, WHEN matched, THEN it is the two vanilla windows and nothing like them
- GIVEN any verdict, WHEN reported, THEN the reason and the verdict always agree

### ChatResponseTransformerTests.cs

_Documents the defensive net that lets CR read a STREAMED (Server-Sent-Events) chat response. The flagship case is the reported one: a player on Chub / Mars got "Failed to parse response: Unexpected character ... value: d" because the endpoint streamed "data: {chunk}" lines instead of one JSON object, even with stream:false._

- GIVEN a streamed sse body, WHEN normalizing, THEN the chunks are reassembled into one message
- GIVEN a normal single json object, WHEN normalizing, THEN it is left untouched
- GIVEN a streamed body carrying full messages, WHEN normalizing, THEN message content is used
- GIVEN sse with blank lines done markers and one malformed chunk, WHEN normalizing, THEN they are skipped
- GIVEN empty or null or whitespace, WHEN normalizing, THEN it is not treated as streamed
- GIVEN an sse shaped body with no parseable data, WHEN normalizing, THEN it falls through to the caller

### ChatWindowLivenessPolicyTests.cs

_when ChatManager may refuse to open a conversation because one is already open, and when that same recorded window must instead be treated as a leftover and cleared._

- GIVEN a window that is genuinely open, WHEN opening again, THEN the request is refused
- GIVEN a layer left on a screen that is gone, WHEN opening, THEN it is a ghost and never refuses
- GIVEN a layer still listed on a screen that has died, WHEN opening, THEN it is a ghost not a window
- GIVEN either half of liveness is missing, WHEN opening, THEN nothing is refused
- GIVEN no window recorded, WHEN opening, THEN it neither refuses nor cleans
- GIVEN any state, WHEN asked, THEN refusing and ghosting are never both true

### ChronicleCalendarTests.cs

_Protects the year-grouping math shared by the text chronicle and the illuminated viewer: a drift here would split or merge chapters inconsistently between the two rendering paths._

- GIVEN day zero, WHEN computing the year, THEN it is the first year
- GIVEN the last day of the first year, WHEN computing the year, THEN it is still the first year
- GIVEN the first day of the second year, WHEN computing the year, THEN it rolls over
- GIVEN several years in, WHEN computing the year, THEN it matches simple division
- GIVEN a day in each quarter of the year, WHEN naming the season, THEN it walks spring to winter
- GIVEN the first day of a new year, WHEN naming the season, THEN it is spring again
- GIVEN the first and last days of a year, WHEN computing the day of year, THEN it runs 1 to 84
- GIVEN ordinal edge numbers, WHEN formatting, THEN the suffixes are correct

### ClaimantSupportPolicyTests.cs

_Council Tier 4 (support_claimant): this policy is the decision SettleSupportClaimant used to make inline, now pure and testable. What breaks if it is wrong: a hostile or ineligible vassal appears to back the player (moving real clan influence they should not), or a genuine backer is turned away. The gate ORDER matters (a dead backer is "not found" before anything else is even asked), and a zero/negative relation must yield no backing so a cold vassal can never publicly support the player. Exact influence amounts are CourtActionPolicy's own tested job; these tests pin the outcomes and the "positive relation backs, non-positive does not" boundary._

- GIVEN an eligible well liked backer, THEN the backing is applied with a positive amount
- GIVEN a dead backer, THEN the outcome is not found even if otherwise eligible
- GIVEN a backer who is not a lord or is a prisoner, THEN the outcome is not found
- GIVEN no clan or the players own clan, THEN the outcome is backer is player house
- GIVEN a backer outside the players kingdom, THEN the outcome is not in player kingdom
- GIVEN a non positive relation, THEN the backing is refused as unaffordable
- GIVEN a willing backer with no influence, THEN the backing is unaffordable

### CoerciveSceneEventPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the line between coercion and intimacy in the memory model. A captive/captor scene is non-consensual; if a romantic [EVENT] from one were stored as consensual intimacy, the victim would "have intimacy with" their abuser, it would grant the intimacy regard gain, and it could rouse jealousy as courtship. This policy is what stops that. If it regresses, rape starts reading as a love affair in the NPC's memory._

- GIVEN a coercive scene, WHEN the event is Intimacy, THEN it is dropped
- GIVEN a coercive scene, WHEN the event is Flirt, THEN it is dropped
- GIVEN a coercive scene, WHEN the event is not romantic, THEN it is kept
- GIVEN a non coercive scene, WHEN the event is Intimacy, THEN it is kept
- GIVEN no event, WHEN asked, THEN nothing is dropped

### CommitmentJournalTextTests.cs

_Documents the exact wording of the pledge (TrackedCommitment) quest-journal mirror: one behaviour per lifecycle transition, plus the fallback names when a hero reference is unavailable._

- GIVEN a promisor name, WHEN building the title, THEN it reads Pledge colon name
- GIVEN no promisor name, WHEN building the title, THEN it falls back to they
- GIVEN a promisor and rival, WHEN the pledge is opened, THEN the line reports the vow
- GIVEN no rival name, WHEN the pledge is opened, THEN it falls back to their rival
- GIVEN a promisor and rival, WHEN the pledge is fulfilled, THEN the line reports the kept word
- GIVEN a promisor and rival, WHEN the pledge is breached, THEN the line reports the broken oath
- GIVEN the active state, WHEN building a log line, THEN it reads as the opened line
- GIVEN the fulfilled state, WHEN building a log line, THEN it reads as the fulfilled line
- GIVEN the breached state, WHEN building a log line, THEN it reads as the breached line
- GIVEN a pay gold oath, WHEN the oath is opened, THEN the line names the sum
- GIVEN a keep peace oath, WHEN the oath is fulfilled, THEN the line names the faction
- GIVEN a protect oath, WHEN the oath is breached, THEN the line reads without a target
- GIVEN no promisor name, WHEN a pay gold oath is opened, THEN it falls back to they
- GIVEN a pay gold oath with no amount, WHEN opened, THEN it still reads as a plain sentence
- GIVEN the active state, WHEN building a kind aware log line, THEN it reads as the opened line

### CommitmentPolicyTests.cs

_Pure math behind a TrackedCommitment (a lord's PLEDGE to move against a rival): when its deadline falls, and how much it moves relation/stance when honored versus broken. CommitmentBehavior applies these numbers straight to the player's regard with the lord (StanceService.OnPromiseKept / OnPromiseBroken) and to the daily overdue sweep that decides whether a pledge is still alive, so a wrong deadline either lets a broken vow slide forever or breaches a pledge the lord hasn't had time to keep, and a wrong magnitude either lets an empty promise cost nothing or rewards a kept one more than breaking one stings._

- GIVEN an issued day, WHEN computing the deadline, THEN it is one season later
- GIVEN an issued day of zero, WHEN computing the deadline, THEN it is just the season length
- GIVEN the deadline not yet reached, WHEN checking overdue, THEN it is not overdue
- GIVEN the deadline reached exactly, WHEN checking overdue, THEN the threshold is inclusive
- GIVEN well past the deadline, WHEN checking overdue, THEN it is overdue
- GIVEN the fulfilled bonus, WHEN read, THEN it is a positive reward
- GIVEN the breached penalty, WHEN read, THEN it is a negative penalty
- GIVEN the breach and fulfillment magnitudes, WHEN compared, THEN breaking a promise stings harder than keeping one rewards
- GIVEN a kind needing a positive act, WHEN the deadline lapses with nothing verified, THEN it is a breach
- GIVEN keep peace, WHEN the deadline lapses with no war declared, THEN it is a fulfillment
- GIVEN zero witnesses, WHEN computing the public oath penalty, THEN it adds nothing
- GIVEN one witness, WHEN computing the public oath penalty, THEN it matches the per witness rate
- GIVEN enough witnesses to exceed the floor, WHEN computing the public oath penalty, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a negative witness count, WHEN computing the public oath penalty, THEN it adds nothing
- GIVEN an amount over the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it is capped to the gold pledge ceiling
- GIVEN an ordinary amount, WHEN clamped, THEN it passes through unchanged
- GIVEN a non positive amount, WHEN clamped, THEN it resolves to zero
- GIVEN the per witness penalty, WHEN read, THEN it is negative
- GIVEN the broken oath grudge magnitude, WHEN read, THEN it is positive
- GIVEN a calm world, WHEN a breach bound kind lapses, THEN it still breaches
- GIVEN the promisor is a prisoner, WHEN a breach bound kind lapses, THEN it suspends
- GIVEN the player is a prisoner, WHEN a protect oath lapses, THEN it suspends
- GIVEN only the player is a prisoner, WHEN a pay gold oath lapses, THEN it still breaches
- GIVEN the move against rival is gone, WHEN the pledge lapses, THEN it is a neutral void
- GIVEN the rival is gone AND the promisor is captive, WHEN the pledge lapses, THEN void wins over suspend
- GIVEN keep peace, WHEN the deadline lapses even with a captive promisor, THEN it still fulfills
- GIVEN the captivity suspend grace, WHEN read, THEN it is a positive forward window

### CommonerChatContextTests.cs

_Pins the eligibility rule for the "What's the news here?" idle-chat option (CommonsDialogBehavior): who gets offered a slim, memoryless commoner conversation and who does not._

- GIVEN a bandit hideout captain, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a merchant, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a named hero conversation, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a tavern keeper, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a townsperson but not in a civilian settlement, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a townsperson whose occupation casing differs, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is still offered
- GIVEN a villager, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is offered
- GIVEN an anonymous townsperson in a town with the ai ready, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is offered
- GIVEN no conversation partner, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN the ai is unavailable, WHEN evaluated, THEN idle chat is not offered
- GIVEN a townsfolk dependent quest is active, WHEN asked, THEN idle chat is not offered

### CompactTranscriptFoldTests.cs

_that a Compact conversation folds its raw transcript sooner than a Full one._

- GIVEN compact mode, WHEN the fold threshold is asked for, THEN it is tighter than the shipped one
- GIVEN a conversation of eight lines, WHEN planned, THEN compact folds it and full leaves it raw
- GIVEN compact mode, WHEN a long conversation folds, THEN the newest lines are still left raw
- GIVEN a conversation barely started, WHEN planned, THEN neither level folds it

### CompanionAbuseConsequencePolicyTests.cs

_Companion-victim captive scenes, Phase 3: computes the mechanical AFTERMATH once a scene closes, the bond damage the host applies to the companion's Trust/Affection toward the player, the revenge grudge magnitude against a NAMED captor, and the world-event/chronicle weight. This is the payoff of the whole companion-scene pillar: it turns a narrated ordeal into a lasting, verified consequence the player actually feels (a companion who trusts them less, a grudge that can be avenged, an atrocity that gets talked about). Get the severity-0 short-circuit wrong and a benign scene wrongly scars a bond; get the coerced/named branches wrong and the player either escapes a real betrayal's cost, or a faceless bandit wrongly earns a lasting vendetta._

- GIVEN a non abusive scene severity zero, WHEN resolving, THEN no consequence forms
- GIVEN an abusive scene, WHEN resolving, THEN the bond craters and news carries
- GIVEN a faceless bandit captor, WHEN resolving, THEN no lasting revenge grudge forms
- GIVEN a named captor, WHEN resolving, THEN a revenge grudge forms and deepens with severity
- GIVEN the player was forced to do it, WHEN resolving, THEN the bond craters deeper than merely watching

### CompanionAffectionThresholdTests.cs

_how deep a bond a companion needs before they dare confess they have feelings for the player, and how that threshold follows the Relationship Pacing the player chose._

- GIVEN the grounded pacing, WHEN asking the threshold, THEN it is exactly what shipped
- GIVEN a warmer pacing, WHEN asking the threshold, THEN the confession comes within reach
- GIVEN the most generous pacing, WHEN asking the threshold, THEN it is still a real bond
- GIVEN a bond between the two thresholds, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN only the warmer pacing admits it
- GIVEN a companion who just cleared the bar, WHEN rolling, THEN the curve starts the same everywhere
- GIVEN a bond below every threshold, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN nobody confides
- GIVEN any pacing, WHEN a companion may confess, THEN the bond they offer can also be formed

### CompanionAmbitionPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the AMBITION found-topic selection: which of the four bound ambitions (a command to lead, a post in the party, a fief to hold, or the player's blessing to wed) a content companion voices, if any. Get the happiness gate wrong and a miserable companion could be made to voice a hopeful ambition, which the design brief calls a grievance in disguise; get the cooldown wrong and a refused ambition nags the player every settlement visit; get the priority wrong and a companion who qualifies for several ambitions at once voices the wrong one, drifting the story away from the rarer, weightier opportunity the design intends to lead with._

- GIVEN a content companion with no field party, WHEN selected, THEN command is chosen
- GIVEN a content companion who holds no party role, WHEN selected, THEN post is chosen
- GIVEN a landless high standing content companion, WHEN selected, THEN fief is chosen
- GIVEN a landless but NOT high standing companion, WHEN selected, THEN fief is not chosen
- GIVEN a high standing but NOT landless companion, WHEN selected, THEN fief is not chosen
- GIVEN a content unmarried companion with a plausible match, WHEN selected, THEN marriage is chosen
- GIVEN no ambition condition holds, WHEN selected, THEN the answer is none
- GIVEN a companion who is not content, WHEN every condition otherwise qualifies, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN a score exactly at the content band, WHEN asked, THEN is content enough agrees with the band
- GIVEN a companion asked within the cooldown window, WHEN selected, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN a companion asked exactly at the cooldown edge, WHEN selected, THEN it is no longer withheld
- GIVEN fief and every other ambition qualify, WHEN selected, THEN fief wins
- GIVEN marriage post and command qualify but not fief, WHEN selected, THEN marriage wins
- GIVEN post and command qualify but not fief or marriage, WHEN selected, THEN post wins
- GIVEN every declared strength, WHEN checked, THEN each stays within 0 100 and below scheme warning
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN selected, THEN the answer is none

### CompanionAudiencePacingTests.cs

_Player report (Nexus, 2026-09-08): companions travelling with the player never say anything unless the player remembers to routinely engage them, because the found-topic audience only ever offered on SettlementEntered. Gabriel's ruling adds a road trigger and makes the whole pacing player-tunable, mirroring InterceptionPacingRules (see that class's own tests for the lord equivalent)._

- GIVEN a player who never opens the menu, WHEN the cooldown is read, THEN it is the shipped 14 days
- GIVEN a player who wants silence, WHEN set to Never, THEN neither the road nor the settlement trigger ever fires
- GIVEN any setting that is not Never, WHEN asked, THEN the feature is live
- GIVEN the four settings, WHEN ordered, THEN each one up genuinely means a chattier road
- GIVEN more company is asked for, WHEN the cooldown is read, THEN it moves WITH the chance not against it
- GIVEN the dial is set to never, WHEN asked, THEN the feature reports itself off

### CompanionAudienceTopicPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the found-topic selection at the heart of the companion-audience feature: the ratified design rule is that an audience must carry something the player can ACT on, DECIDE, or LEARN, and the mod must FIND that thing BEFORE it ever asks for one ("no topic found means no audience, silence is the default"). This is the ONE place that rule is enforced in code: get it wrong and either an ineligible topic gets voiced (the player hears counsel that is false, or a rumour nobody could have heard), or silence stops being the default and a companion starts popping up "just to say something", the exact rival-mod annoyance the owner tested against._

- GIVEN a candidates precondition is false, WHEN selected, THEN it is never chosen
- GIVEN no candidate qualifies, WHEN selected, THEN the answer is none
- GIVEN an empty candidate list, WHEN selected, THEN the answer is none
- GIVEN several eligible candidates, WHEN selected, THEN the strongest wins
- GIVEN a tie in strength, WHEN selected, THEN the answer does not depend on list order
- GIVEN the global cooldown constant, WHEN read, THEN it governs the whole roster not one companion

### CompanionBaselineTests.cs

_Documents the design call that serving a great house is an HONOUR: the happiness a companion comes to rest at is not fixed, it rises with the standing of the clan they serve._

- GIVEN a clan with no standing, WHEN resting, THEN the companion settles barely content
- GIVEN a great house, WHEN resting, THEN the companion settles DEVOTED simply to belong
- GIVEN rising clan tiers, WHEN resting, THEN each tier lifts the resting happiness
- GIVEN an unexpected tier, WHEN resting, THEN the baseline stays within sane bounds

### CompanionCallbackPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the CALLBACK found-topic, flagged in the request as the highest-value topic of the five: the mod already stores every NotableEvent but nothing ever pays one off, and this decides which stored event (if any) is old and strong enough to resurface unprompted. Get MinDaysAgo wrong and a "callback" reads as the mod nagging about last week; get IsSignificant wrong and a throwaway Farewell or FirstMeeting could headline an audience where a real Betrayal or Intimacy should have._

- GIVEN a compressor protected event type, WHEN asked, THEN it is significant
- GIVEN a collaboration event, WHEN asked, THEN it is also significant
- GIVEN a first meeting or flirt event, WHEN asked, THEN it is not significant
- GIVEN a significant event that is too recent, WHEN scored, THEN it does not qualify
- GIVEN a significant event at the age floor, WHEN scored, THEN it qualifies
- GIVEN an insignificant event however old, WHEN scored, THEN it never qualifies
- GIVEN two significant events of different ages, WHEN scored, THEN the older one scores higher
- GIVEN an event far beyond the age bonus ceiling, WHEN scored, THEN it scores the same as at the ceiling
- GIVEN a qualifying event, WHEN asked via qualifies, THEN it agrees with strength

### CompanionContentmentBalanceTests.cs

_The BALANCE of the contentment loop, simulated week by week. The player already has a realm to run: they must not spend their evenings nursing companion morale (design call, 2026-07-13, after SuperGraham339 reported companions sliding into discontent no matter what he promised, until he disabled the system outright)._

- GIVEN the tuning, WHEN compared, THEN the weekly recovery outweighs the ennui of idleness
- GIVEN a merely idle companion, WHEN two years pass, THEN they rest at their clans baseline and never want to leave
- GIVEN an idle companion of a great house, WHEN two years pass, THEN they rest DEVOTED
- GIVEN a companion who started low, WHEN left idle in a proud clan, THEN they CLIMB back to contentment
- GIVEN a companion kept from their spouse, WHEN it is never addressed, THEN they sour but only over many months
- GIVEN a soured companion, WHEN the grievance is righted, THEN they recover
- GIVEN a single slight, WHEN time passes, THEN it is forgiven within weeks

### CompanionCounselPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the PRACTICAL COUNSEL found-topic. The owner's own hard rule governs every test here: "a companion advising the player to vary their food when they already carry eight kinds is worse than silence", the counsel MUST be true when spoken, computed from live state only. Get any of these branches wrong and the mod puts stale or false advice in a companion's mouth, exactly the failure the design rule exists to forbid._

- GIVEN a well stocked party, WHEN selected, THEN no counsel fires
- GIVEN food kinds below the monotony threshold, WHEN selected, THEN food monotony fires
- GIVEN zero food kinds, WHEN selected, THEN food monotony does not fire
- GIVEN morale at partymoralemoods low bar, WHEN selected, THEN low morale fires
- GIVEN morale just above the low bar, WHEN selected, THEN low morale does not fire
- GIVEN wounded troops but a surgeon present, WHEN selected, THEN wounded no surgeon does not fire
- GIVEN wounded troops and no surgeon, WHEN selected, THEN wounded no surgeon fires
- GIVEN carried weight at capacity, WHEN selected, THEN overloaded fires
- GIVEN an unavailable capacity fact, WHEN selected, THEN overloaded never fires
- GIVEN several conditions hold at once, WHEN selected, THEN the strongest one wins
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN selected, THEN no counsel fires

### CompanionEngagementPolicyTests.cs

_Documents what counts as being employed in the player's service. The flagship case is the reported one: a companion made GOVERNOR was still treated as idle and took the ennui penalty week after week._

- GIVEN a companion made governor, WHEN asked, THEN they are NOT idle
- GIVEN a companion leading a party of your clan, WHEN asked, THEN they are NOT idle
- GIVEN a companion away on an errand, WHEN asked, THEN they are NOT idle
- GIVEN a companion with no charge at all, WHEN asked, THEN they are idle

### CompanionErrandJournalTextTests.cs

_Documents the exact wording of the companion-errand quest-journal mirror: one behaviour per phase / closing outcome, plus the per-errand-type verb used on the Outbound line._

- GIVEN a companion name, WHEN building the title, THEN it reads Errand colon name
- GIVEN no companion name, WHEN building the title, THEN it falls back to your companion
- GIVEN a gather news errand, WHEN outbound, THEN the line says bring back news
- GIVEN a spy errand, WHEN outbound, THEN the line says scout it
- GIVEN a steal errand, WHEN outbound, THEN the line says steal something worth bringing home
- GIVEN a barter errand, WHEN outbound, THEN the line says trade for a profit
- GIVEN an envoy errand, WHEN outbound, THEN the line says carry your word as an envoy
- GIVEN no destination name, WHEN outbound, THEN it falls back to a town
- GIVEN any errand, WHEN lingering, THEN the line reports arrival
- GIVEN any errand, WHEN returning, THEN the line reports heading back
- GIVEN any errand, WHEN awaiting reunion, THEN the line reports the captivity wait
- GIVEN a completed errand, WHEN closing as returned, THEN the line reports the homecoming
- GIVEN a recall with a reason, WHEN closing as recalled, THEN the reason is appended in parentheses
- GIVEN a recall with no reason, WHEN closing as recalled, THEN no parenthetical is added

### CompanionGratitudePolicyTests.cs

_Protects the GRATITUDE found-topic: a content companion thanking the player for a specific kindness, purely warm, never a request or a bargain (design brief). Get MinEffectiveMagnitude wrong and every trivial gift pulls the player aside for a whole audience; get RecencyWindowDays wrong and a companion thanks the player for something from many months ago, which reads as delayed rather than heartfelt; get IsContentEnough wrong and an unhappy companion could be made to voice a false thank-you, which the design brief explicitly calls "wrong"._

- GIVEN a significant recent appreciation and a content companion, WHEN checked, THEN it holds
- GIVEN a magnitude below the floor, WHEN checked, THEN it does not hold
- GIVEN a magnitude exactly at the floor, WHEN checked, THEN it holds
- GIVEN an appreciation older than the recency window, WHEN checked, THEN it does not hold
- GIVEN an appreciation exactly at the recency window edge, WHEN checked, THEN it still holds
- GIVEN a companion who is not content, WHEN checked, THEN it does not hold even with a strong appreciation
- GIVEN a score exactly at the content band, WHEN asked, THEN is content enough agrees with the band
- GIVEN two appreciations of different magnitude, WHEN scored, THEN the bigger kindness scores higher
- GIVEN an out of range magnitude, WHEN scored, THEN strength stays clamped to 0 100

### CompanionHappinessTests.cs

_Protects the companion-happiness model — clamping, the band thresholds, the "wants to leave" floor, the per-driver direction, and the gentle drift back to baseline (recovery without overshoot)._

- GIVEN a delta past the bounds, WHEN applied, THEN the score is clamped
- GIVEN scores at each threshold, WHEN banded, THEN they read as expected
- GIVEN a score at or below the floor, WHEN asked, THEN they want to leave
- GIVEN a welcome driver, WHEN applied, THEN happiness rises
- GIVEN an unwelcome driver, WHEN applied, THEN happiness falls
- GIVEN being taken prisoner, WHEN compared to a failed errand, THEN capture stings more
- GIVEN a score above baseline, WHEN it drifts, THEN it eases down toward default
- GIVEN a score below baseline, WHEN it drifts, THEN it eases up toward default
- GIVEN a score near baseline, WHEN it drifts, THEN it lands on default without overshoot

### CompanionLifecyclePolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.2 companion hardening. CompanionLifecyclePolicy gates two lifecycle transitions the engine drives: - MayDepartNow feeds CompanionHappinessBehavior / WarWearinessBehavior: a companion AWAY on an errand must be excluded from the organic quit/retire loops, or one abroad on the player's business departs mid-errand._

- GIVEN a companion away on an errand, WHEN asked if they may depart now, THEN no
- GIVEN a companion at home, WHEN asked if they may depart now, THEN yes
- GIVEN a still seated clan companion, WHEN asked if they may reintegrate, THEN yes
- GIVEN a companion expelled while away, WHEN asked if they may reintegrate, THEN no
- GIVEN a companion no longer of the players clan, WHEN asked if they may reintegrate, THEN no

### CompanionMissionsTests.cs

_Protects the companion-mission decision logic — the governing skill / risk per errand, the attempt floor, and the deterministic success bands (incl. capture on a botched risky errand, the loop-closer into prisoner rescue). Pure foundation, ahead of the engine glue._

- GIVEN each errand, WHEN asked for its skill, THEN it maps to the expected one
- GIVEN each errand, WHEN asked for its payload, THEN it maps to the expected kind
- GIVEN a spy or steal errand, WHEN asked, THEN it is risky
- GIVEN a news barter or envoy errand, WHEN asked, THEN it is not risky
- GIVEN a green companion, WHEN below the skill floor, THEN the errand is refused
- GIVEN high skill a good roll and low difficulty, WHEN resolved, THEN it fully succeeds
- GIVEN a middling total, WHEN resolved, THEN it partly succeeds
- GIVEN a weak total on a safe errand, WHEN resolved, THEN it fails without capture
- GIVEN a botched risky errand, WHEN the score bottoms out, THEN the companion is captured
- GIVEN a risky errand that merely falls short, WHEN above the capture line, THEN it fails but the agent returns
- GIVEN out of range inputs, WHEN resolved, THEN they are clamped not thrown
- GIVEN a quick errand at no difficulty, WHEN timed, THEN it takes the base days
- GIVEN a farther errand, WHEN timed, THEN it runs a day longer than a quick one
- GIVEN rising difficulty, WHEN timed, THEN each band adds a day on the road
- GIVEN a full success, WHEN sizing the report, THEN it carries a full briefing
- GIVEN a partial, WHEN sizing the report, THEN it carries a thinner briefing
- GIVEN a failure, WHEN sizing the report, THEN it carries nothing
- GIVEN fewer items available than the cap, WHEN sizing, THEN it never promises more than exist
- GIVEN a clean steal, WHEN scaling loot, THEN it brings the full haul
- GIVEN a partial steal, WHEN scaling loot, THEN it brings a fraction
- GIVEN a failed steal, WHEN scaling loot, THEN it brings nothing
- GIVEN a nonpositive base, WHEN scaling loot, THEN it brings nothing

### CompanionPositiveDriversTests.cs

_Documents the deeds of ordinary play now counting toward companion contentment: victories, the camp's morale, posts handed out, and being genuinely heard. Before this, a companion could win your wars and govern your city and still sour, because only the mod's own errands ever lifted them (player request, SuperGraham339)._

- GIVEN a battle fought at your side, WHEN it is won or lost, THEN the companion shares the outcome
- GIVEN a defeat, WHEN a single week of drift passes, THEN it is already forgiven
- GIVEN a companion handed a post, WHEN appointed, THEN the mark of trust lifts them most
- GIVEN a victorious and well led warlord, WHEN the season goes well, THEN companions rise ABOVE their clans rest
- GIVEN a camp in good spirits, WHEN the week turns, THEN the mood lifts the companions
- GIVEN a sullen hungry camp, WHEN the week turns, THEN the mood dulls the companions
- GIVEN an unremarkable camp, WHEN the week turns, THEN the mood says nothing at all
- GIVEN a companion never talked to, WHEN the player sits with them, THEN it lifts their spirits
- GIVEN a player reopening the chat at once, WHEN asked, THEN it cannot be farmed
- GIVEN the cooldown fully elapsed, WHEN the player sits with them again, THEN it lifts them again
- GIVEN a clock that ran backwards, WHEN asked, THEN the companion is not locked out forever

### CompanionRapportTests.cs

_Protects the camp-life rapport model — trait compatibility (bond vs clash), the relation bands, the "worth raising with the player" threshold, and the companion-marriage warmth gate._

- GIVEN two honourable companions, WHEN compared, THEN they bond
- GIVEN an honourable and a dishonourable companion, WHEN compared, THEN they clash
- GIVEN a neutral axis, WHEN compared, THEN it neither bonds nor grates
- GIVEN two fully aligned companions, WHEN compared, THEN compatibility sums every weight
- GIVEN two fully opposed companions, WHEN compared, THEN compatibility is the negated sum
- GIVEN relations at each threshold, WHEN banded, THEN they read as expected
- GIVEN a strong feeling either way, WHEN asked, THEN it is worth raising
- GIVEN two warm eligible companions, WHEN asked, THEN they may wed
- GIVEN any failed precondition, WHEN asked, THEN they may not wed

### CompanionRescueTests.cs

_Protects the companion-rescue odds: no companions means no rescue, the daily chance scales with how many can ride, it is capped, and a roll inside the chance triggers the attempt._

- GIVEN no free companions, WHEN asked, THEN no chance and no attempt
- GIVEN more companions, WHEN asked, THEN the chance rises but is capped
- GIVEN a roll inside the chance, WHEN checked, THEN the rescue is attempted
- GIVEN a roll at or above the chance, WHEN checked, THEN no rescue this day

### CompanionRetentionTests.cs

_Protects the companion-retention loop: an unhappy companion asks for an audience, a granted audience clears the grievance, refusals accumulate, and after the cap the companion leaves by farewell — never silently._

- GIVEN contentment disabled, WHEN decided, THEN nothing happens even if unhappy
- GIVEN a content companion, WHEN decided, THEN nothing happens
- GIVEN an unhappy companion below the cap, WHEN decided, THEN they ask for an audience
- GIVEN the refusal cap is reached, WHEN decided, THEN they send a farewell
- GIVEN a refused audience, WHEN registered, THEN the count rises by one
- GIVEN three refusals in a row, WHEN decided, THEN only the fourth decide leaves
- GIVEN refusals built up, WHEN an audience is granted, THEN the count resets
- GIVEN the cap, WHEN asked, THEN they are out of patience

### CompanionRumourPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the RUMOUR REPORT found-topic's strength scoring. WorldEventStore already decides WHETHER a companion could plausibly have heard an event (RumorAwareness) and how surely (RumorConfidence); this only turns that existing rating into the shared 0..100 scale the other found-topics compete on, so a rumour is neither an automatic winner nor an automatic loser against a callback or a grudge._

- GIVEN the same magnitude, WHEN confidence differs, THEN well known scores higher than distant
- GIVEN a high magnitude, WHEN well known, THEN strength never exceeds 100
- GIVEN a low magnitude, WHEN distant, THEN strength never goes below zero

### CompanionScenePolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a captive encounter targets a held companion (only when one is held, a roll inside the chance) and how the variant is drawn (coerce the player / the men assault / the captor torments)._

- GIVEN no companion is held, WHEN deciding, THEN the scene never targets a companion
- GIVEN a companion is held, WHEN the roll lands, THEN the scene targets the companion
- GIVEN a companion is held, WHEN the roll misses, THEN the player remains the victim
- GIVEN a low roll, WHEN picking the variant, THEN the player is coerced to act
- GIVEN a mid roll, WHEN picking the variant, THEN the men assault collectively
- GIVEN a high roll, WHEN picking the variant, THEN the captor torments in person

### CompanionSchemeWarningPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the SCHEME WARNING found-topic: a loyal companion warning the player that someone is secretly plotting against them (design brief). Get IsContentEnough wrong and a disaffected companion could be made to voice a warning they would not, in truth, go out of their way to deliver; get Strength too low and a live threat to the player could lose the audience to a mere thank-you or rumour, exactly what the "high-value, urgent" design intent rules out._

- GIVEN a content companion, WHEN checked, THEN it holds
- GIVEN a companion who is not content, WHEN checked, THEN it does not hold
- GIVEN a score exactly at the content band, WHEN asked, THEN is content enough agrees with the band
- GIVEN a typical gratitude strength, WHEN compared, THEN scheme warning outranks it
- GIVEN the pinned strength, WHEN checked, THEN it stays within 0 100

### CompanionSecretAffectionPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the eligibility and weekly-roll rules CompanionAffectionBehavior consults before a companion asks for a private word to confide love and become the player's SECRET LOVER (v1.30.3, "never a bargain, kept hidden to spare the household jealousy"). Wrongly eligible means an incompatible, unhappy, already-attached, or shallow-bond companion propositions the player out of nowhere; too strict means a genuinely deep, faithful bond never gets its payoff. The weekly-chance scaling and its hard cap decide how quickly that payoff arrives without ever feeling guaranteed._

- GIVEN all conditions hold, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN the companion could confide
- GIVEN the hero is not a companion, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN an incompatible orientation, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN adult content is disabled, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN the companion is not content, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN the companion is already attached, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN the bond is below the minimum regard, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN they cannot confide
- GIVEN a regard, WHEN computing the weekly chance, THEN it scales and clamps
- GIVEN regard sixty with a chance of twenty five, WHEN rolling, THEN only the bottom quarter confides
- GIVEN a negative roll, WHEN deciding, THEN the companion does not confide

### CompetitionSelectorTests.cs

_The v1.5 competition loop (JealousyBehavior.EvaluateCompetition): once the player keeps several sharing-culture partners, this picks AT MOST ONE to feel neglected on a given pass, or none._

- GIVEN a household all recently seen, WHEN selecting, THEN no one competes
- GIVEN a neglected but monogamous partner, WHEN selecting, THEN they do not merely compete
- GIVEN a partner just past the floor but within the inequity gap, WHEN selecting, THEN they do not react
- GIVEN everyone neglected evenly, WHEN selecting, THEN no one feels singled out
- GIVEN fewer than two partners, WHEN selecting, THEN no one competes
- GIVEN one partner neglected far more than the favoured one, WHEN selecting, THEN they compete
- GIVEN several eligible partners, WHEN selecting, THEN the most neglected wins
- GIVEN the only eligible partner is on cooldown, WHEN selecting, THEN they are skipped
- GIVEN the most neglected partner is open, WHEN selecting, THEN a different non open partner is chosen
- GIVEN the only neglected partner is open, WHEN selecting, THEN no one competes

### ConceptionChroniclePolicyTests.cs

_CHRONICLE: whether a conception the player is party to is chronicled PUBLICLY (romance audit C5). This gate is the last line before a conception becomes a PlayerFeat that NPCs, tavern rumours, and the player's spouse all learn. It protects the whole bastard pillar: get it wrong in one direction and an adulterous conception leaks the day it happens (secret dead, "an heir" said of a bastard); get it wrong the other way and a legitimate heir's begetting is never recorded. Both are silent, hard-to-notice regressions, so they are pinned here._

- GIVEN an adulterous conception, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not a public heir
- GIVEN a legitimate conception with a lord spouse, WHEN deciding, THEN it is a public heir
- GIVEN a spouse who is not a lord, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not a public heir

### ConceptionResolverTests.cs

_Protects the conception policy: who (if anyone) becomes the mother of a vaginal act. The "same-sex / no fertile female → no child" rule and the fertile window are the whole guarantee — a drift would silently conceive impossible children or deny valid ones._

- GIVEN a fertile female player and a living male npc, WHEN resolving, THEN the player is the mother
- GIVEN a male player and a fertile female npc, WHEN resolving, THEN the npc is the mother
- GIVEN two females, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception for lack of a father
- GIVEN two males, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception for lack of a mother
- GIVEN a female player below the fertile window, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception
- GIVEN a female npc above the fertile window, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception
- GIVEN the fertile window bounds, WHEN checking the mother, THEN eighteen and fortyfive are inclusive
- GIVEN a dead fertile female, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception
- GIVEN a fertile female npc and a dead male player, WHEN resolving, THEN no conception for lack of a living father

### ConsoleHelpPolicyTests.cs

_that cr.help can never again omit a command, or name one that does not exist, and that reading 259 of them stays possible._

- GIVEN the whole registry, WHEN listing everything, THEN not one command is left out
- GIVEN commands that take arguments, WHEN listing, THEN the arguments are shown with them
- GIVEN a command nobody wrote a line for, WHEN checked, THEN it is reported as undocumented
- GIVEN an entry with no words in it, WHEN checked, THEN it counts as undocumented
- GIVEN a documented command that was renamed away, WHEN checked, THEN it is reported as dead
- GIVEN the curated prose names a command that is gone, WHEN checked, THEN it is reported too
- GIVEN the curated page, WHEN closed, THEN it says how many there are and how to reach them
- GIVEN an exact command name, WHEN asked, THEN it answers in full rather than listing
- GIVEN a name that is also the stem of a family, WHEN asked, THEN the relatives are named too
- GIVEN a name with no relatives, WHEN asked, THEN nothing is invented to list beside it
- GIVEN a name in any case, WHEN asked, THEN it is still recognised
- GIVEN a fragment, WHEN searching, THEN each match comes with what it does
- GIVEN a long description, WHEN listed in a search, THEN only its first sentence is shown
- GIVEN prose with no sentence end, WHEN shortened, THEN it cuts at a word and says it was cut
- GIVEN more matches than the threshold, WHEN searching, THEN it answers with names alone
- GIVEN a fragment matching nothing, WHEN searching, THEN it points at the full list
- GIVEN an empty fragment, WHEN searching, THEN everything is listed
- GIVEN a ragged registry, WHEN listing, THEN it is tidied rather than shown as it is
- GIVEN a registry in any order, WHEN listing, THEN it comes out alphabetical
- GIVEN an empty registry, WHEN listing, THEN it says so instead of showing nothing
- GIVEN a command with no entry, WHEN listing or searching, THEN it is still shown

### ContentmentPacingTests.cs

_The player's chosen pacing for companion contentment. The rebalance made the loop deliberately gentle; this gives the tension back to those who want it (SuperGraham339 liked the idea, he only wanted it tuned). The rule these tests pin above all: WHATEVER the setting, a companion left merely idle can never be carried out of your service. The slider tunes how long a REAL grievance takes to fade, never whether idleness alone can kill._

- GIVEN ANY pacing, WHEN a companion is merely idle, THEN the drift home still outweighs the ennui
- GIVEN ANY pacing, WHEN two years of idleness pass, THEN the companion never wants to leave
- GIVEN the three pacings, WHEN compared, THEN forgiveness comes faster the gentler the setting
- GIVEN the default, WHEN asked, THEN it is the balance the rebalance was tuned around
- GIVEN a FORGIVING lord, WHEN a real grievance is never addressed, THEN they still never leave
- GIVEN an EXACTING lord, WHEN a real grievance is never addressed, THEN they leave SOONER than at balanced

### ConversationCompressionPolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides WHEN a running conversation's older lines get folded into a compressed summary, and WHICH lines. It exists because a long exchange (a council of several members, or any talk that runs on) must keep every turn coherent with the whole conversation without replaying an ever-growing RAW transcript, which inflates every prompt and can overflow a small or reasoning model (Gabriel 2026-07-30). If the fold triggers too eagerly it burns LLM calls on short talks; too late and the transcript is already overflowing. If the "recent kept" boundary is wrong, either a just-said line vanishes into a lossy summary (incoherence, the exact thing this feature prevents) or nothing is ever folded. Each test pins one of those boundaries._

- GIVEN an unfolded count at or below the threshold, WHEN planning, THEN nothing is folded
- GIVEN the unfolded count exceeds the threshold, WHEN planning, THEN the older range is folded
- GIVEN a fold, WHEN planning, THEN exactly the recent kept lines stay out of the fold
- GIVEN some lines already folded, WHEN planning, THEN the fold resumes from that point
- GIVEN all lines beyond the summary are within the recent window, WHEN planning, THEN nothing is folded

### ConversationEndPolicyTests.cs

_ConversationEndPolicy backs the "NPCs can end conversations" MCM toggle. Get it wrong two ways: an ordinary chat that ignores the toggle keeps trapping the player who turned it off (the feature does nothing), or a CAPTIVE scene that obeys the toggle would break its own Continue/resume flow (a player could dodge a scene's pacing). These tests pin both: the ordinary Chatroom follows the setting, a captive/captor scene always locks._

- GIVEN an ordinary conversation, WHEN the npc ends, THEN it locks only if the toggle allows
- GIVEN a captive or captor scene, WHEN the npc ends, THEN it always locks

### ConversationMemoryRulesTests.cs

_Documents when a closing conversation earns a fallback memory: two turns always, one turn only when the player's words carried substance (the reported "NPC forgot our one-exchange conversation" fault)._

- GIVEN two completed turns, WHEN closing, THEN a fallback memory is deserved
- GIVEN one substantial turn, WHEN closing, THEN a fallback memory is deserved
- GIVEN one trivial turn, WHEN closing, THEN no memory spam
- GIVEN no completed turn, WHEN closing, THEN nothing is recorded

### ConversationSummaryQueueTests.cs

_Increment B: the pure logic behind the durable pending-summary queue. When a conversation ends, the mod records a base memory event ("Spoke with X.") and fires an async LLM call meant to replace it with a rich summary; if the game closes or crashes before that call returns, the enrichment plan is lost and the NPC's memory keeps the bare placeholder forever. This queue IS that plan, captured as data durable enough to survive a save/quit: a background worker (a later increment) drains it to actually call the summarizer, and a load-time replay re-enqueues whatever survived the save._

- GIVEN two jobs enqueued, WHEN draining, THEN they come out oldest first
- GIVEN the same anchor key enqueued twice, WHEN enqueuing, THEN only one copy is kept
- GIVEN a conversation resumed and closed again, WHEN its summary is offered, THEN the fuller account takes the place of the shorter one
- GIVEN the job being replaced had already failed, WHEN the fuller one takes its place, THEN it inherits those attempts
- GIVEN the queue is at capacity, WHEN enqueuing one more, THEN the oldest job is dropped and size stays capped
- GIVEN a queued job, WHEN completed, THEN it is removed from the queue
- GIVEN a job fails, WHEN below MaxAttempts, THEN Attempts increments and the job is kept
- GIVEN a job fails a third time, WHEN that reaches MaxAttempts, THEN the job is dropped
- GIVEN a queue with a job, WHEN serializing and deserializing, THEN every field round trips
- GIVEN null input, WHEN deserializing, THEN the result is an empty queue
- GIVEN empty string input, WHEN deserializing, THEN the result is an empty queue
- GIVEN garbage corrupt input, WHEN deserializing, THEN it does not throw and yields an empty queue
- GIVEN a second conversation the same day, WHEN enqueued, THEN it is not mistaken for the first
- GIVEN the same job twice, WHEN enqueued, THEN the repeat is still refused
- GIVEN jobs from an older save, WHEN enqueued, THEN they are still matched by value

### ConversationTranscriptTests.cs

_The transcript is the conversation's single source of truth, and both things read from it are load-bearing: every participant's ChatSession is rebuilt from it before every LLM call, and the memory summarizer is handed a rendering of it to write what the NPC will remember FOREVER. It was a raw List of a class nested privately inside ChatViewModel, so none of it could be tested (the mod is net472, this project is net10) even though not one line of it touches the engine._

- GIVEN a spoken line, WHEN recording, THEN it is kept attributed as given
- GIVEN nothing was actually said, WHEN recording, THEN no line is kept
- GIVEN a narration line, WHEN recording, THEN it is marked as narration
- GIVEN an exchange, WHEN rendering to text, THEN every line is attributed by name
- GIVEN a captive scene label, WHEN rendering to text, THEN the players lines carry it
- GIVEN nothing was said, WHEN rendering to text, THEN the result is empty
- GIVEN an exchange, WHEN composing for one speaker, THEN they receive their own view of it
- GIVEN no summary, WHEN composing, THEN the whole raw history is returned unchanged
- GIVEN a folded summary, WHEN composing, THEN the recap leads and only recent lines follow raw
- GIVEN a backward or oversized marker, WHEN applying a summary, THEN it is clamped
- GIVEN a prior conversation, WHEN seeding a fresh transcript, THEN all its lines carry across in order
- GIVEN a prior conversation that had folded, WHEN seeding, THEN the recap and its marker carry across
- GIVEN a seeded transcript, WHEN composing for a participant, THEN they receive the prior exchange
- GIVEN no prior conversation, WHEN seeding from null, THEN it is a no op

### CorrespondenceInterestTests.cs

_A2's adaptive layer: CorrespondenceInterestPolicy turns the player's correspondence behaviour, implicit (read but ignored, or actually answered) and explicit (a stated preference), into the deltas that eventually reach LetterWeights.WeightWithInterest. Get a sign or a floor wrong here and the mailbox either never learns anything (a damper that does not damp) or, worse, learns to silence a correspondent completely (a floor that is not held), which is exactly the outcome EffectiveWeightFloor exists to forbid._

- GIVEN an acknowledged but unanswered letter, WHEN the delta is computed, THEN sender and subject dip but global is untouched
- GIVEN the player actually replies, WHEN the delta is computed, THEN sender recovers sharply and global is untouched
- GIVEN a sender acknowledged over and over, WHEN clamped after each step, THEN interest reaches but never passes the floor
- GIVEN several acknowledges, WHEN a reply follows, THEN sender interest lifts back toward zero
- GIVEN a stated preference, WHEN the delta is computed, THEN only the scoped channel moves by the named magnitude
- GIVEN CorrespondencePreference None, WHEN the delta is computed, THEN every channel is zero
- GIVEN a value outside range, WHEN ClampSender is applied, THEN it holds the sender floor and ceiling
- GIVEN a value outside range, WHEN ClampSubject is applied, THEN it holds the subject floor and ceiling
- GIVEN a value outside range, WHEN ClampGlobal is applied, THEN it holds the global floor and ceiling

### CorrespondencePreferenceParserTests.cs

_CorrespondencePreferenceParser is the ONLY thing standing between an LLM's loosely-formatted reply and the correspondence-interest dial actually moving. Nearly every reply carries no preference at all, so the absent-marker path is the single most exercised branch and must always be silent (None), never an error._

- GIVEN no marker present, WHEN parsing, THEN the result is None
- GIVEN each recognised scope, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the matching PreferenceScope
- GIVEN each recognised direction, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the matching PreferenceDirection
- GIVEN each recognised strength, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the matching PreferenceStrength
- GIVEN tokens in a reordered sequence, WHEN parsing, THEN the preference still parses correctly
- GIVEN mixed or upper case marker and tokens, WHEN parsing, THEN it still parses case insensitively
- GIVEN extra whitespace and newlines between tokens, WHEN parsing, THEN it still parses correctly
- GIVEN a marker with only a scope, WHEN parsing, THEN direction defaults to Less and strength to Mild
- GIVEN garbled direction and strength tokens, WHEN parsing, THEN they fall back to the same defaults
- GIVEN a marker with no scope at all, WHEN parsing, THEN the whole preference is None
- GIVEN an explicit scope of none, WHEN parsing, THEN the whole preference is None
- GIVEN the marker followed by garbage, WHEN parsing, THEN it degrades to None without throwing

### CouncilAccordClauseTests.cs

_Council Tier 2: these clauses are injected verbatim into an NPC's 1:1 prompt so the character can reference a still-standing council accord ("the tribute I owe you"). What breaks if a clause is wrong: the NPC contradicts the real ledger to the player's face. The single most dangerous error is DIRECTION, an NPC told it RECEIVES a tribute it actually PAYS, or that it was GRANTED a stipend it in fact grants, so most tests here pin who owes whom. The remaining-term wording is pinned too, since "1 day remains" vs "5 days remain" is the one grammatical seam, and a stray "(0 days remain)" would read as a live accord that has actually lapsed._

- GIVEN a tribute, THEN the clause says the NPC owes the player the daily amount
- GIVEN a tribute with missed days, THEN the clause notes falling behind
- GIVEN a tribute with no missed days, THEN the clause does not note falling behind
- GIVEN a stipend, THEN the clause says the player grants it to the NPC
- GIVEN a pact, THEN the clause frames a non aggression pact between the houses
- GIVEN the NPC is the hostage, THEN the clause says they are held by the player
- GIVEN the NPC pledged a hostage, THEN the clause names who their house gave
- GIVEN an unresolved hostage name, THEN the clause falls back to a readable phrase
- GIVEN a blank player name, THEN the clause falls back to the player
- GIVEN a positive remaining term, THEN the clause states it singular aware
- GIVEN a spent remaining term, THEN the clause carries no days parenthetical

### CouncilAccordPanelTextTests.cs

_Council Tier 3: these lines are what the PLAYER reads on a hero's Encyclopedia page ("Owes you a tribute..."), the third-person mirror of the first-person prompt clauses. What breaks if a line is wrong: the page tells the player the opposite of the truth. As with the prompt describer, DIRECTION is the load-bearing fact (a hero that OWES a tribute must not read as RECEIVING one; a ward's mentor and protege sides must not swap), so most tests pin it. The remaining-term wording is pinned too so a live accord is never shown as "(0 days remain)"._

- GIVEN a tribute, THEN the line reads that the hero owes the player
- GIVEN a stipend, THEN the line reads that the hero receives it from the player
- GIVEN a tribute with missed days, THEN the line notes falling behind
- GIVEN a tribute with no missed days, THEN the line does not note falling behind
- GIVEN a pact, THEN the line frames a non aggression pact
- GIVEN the hero is held hostage, THEN the line reads held by you
- GIVEN the hero gave a hostage, THEN the line names who was given
- GIVEN the hero is a ward protege, THEN the line names their mentor
- GIVEN the hero is a ward mentor, THEN the line names their protege
- GIVEN unresolved names, THEN the lines fall back to readable phrases
- GIVEN a ward or a spent term, THEN no days parenthetical is shown
- GIVEN a positive term, THEN the line states it singular aware

### CouncilAnchorPolicyTests.cs

_Decides which seated member speaks the reply when the player addresses a council. Feeds the council session: the chat renders one primary respondent per player turn (Gabriel's whole-table model is ONE call yielding that spoken reply plus a reaction from each other member who speaks or gestures), and this names them._

- GIVEN members of differing regard, WHEN choosing, THEN the warmest answers
- GIVEN two members of equal regard, WHEN the roster order changes, THEN the same one answers
- GIVEN a single seated member, WHEN choosing, THEN they answer
- GIVEN every member resents the player, WHEN choosing, THEN the least hostile answers
- GIVEN nobody is seated, WHEN choosing, THEN there is no anchor

### CouncilPanelPolicyTests.cs

_Council Tier 1 reconciliation: the Engagements panel and the end-of-council memory seal are both driven from the REAL resolution ledger now, so this policy is the single arbiter of what the player sees on the panel and what gets remembered. What breaks if it is wrong: a withdrawn motion lingers on the panel as if still standing; a committed decision shows the wrong status word; or a seal double-writes a memory for a pledge kind that already recorded its own at the lift (SwearOath via CommitmentBehavior, PledgeAgainst via LaunchPledge), so the NPC ends up with two memories of the same act._

- GIVEN a reversed motion, WHEN deciding panel visibility, THEN it is hidden
- GIVEN any non reversed status, WHEN deciding panel visibility, THEN it is shown
- GIVEN a status, WHEN labelling it for the panel, THEN it reads the expected word
- GIVEN a motion that did not commit, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is not sealed
- GIVEN an applied ordinary kind, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is sealed
- GIVEN an applied kind that already records its own memory, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is not sealed
- GIVEN a self memorising kind in odd casing, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is still not sealed

### CouncilSceneImagePolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides which situational SCENE image covers the anchor NPC's portrait on the council's "thinking" placeholder. It feeds straight into the message row's runtime image path (ChatViewModel), so a wrong basename here is a wrong (or missing, file-not-found) image in play. What breaks if it is wrong: the wrong mood art shows during a council turn (e.g. a war council image in a family sitting), or the wrong gendered file is requested and silently fails to load, leaving the ordinary anchor portrait as the only visible fallback._

- GIVEN a generic council outside in desert, THEN the desert image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council outside in steppe, THEN the steppe image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council outside on any other terrain, THEN the forest mountains image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council inside a settlement, THEN the tavern image is chosen
- GIVEN a family council, WHEN the player is female, THEN the female family asset is chosen
- GIVEN a family council, WHEN the player is male, THEN the male family image is chosen
- GIVEN a war council, WHEN male, THEN the male war image is chosen
- GIVEN a war council, WHEN female, THEN the female war image is chosen
- GIVEN a family council but the player is outside, THEN the family image still wins over the outside art
- GIVEN a war council but the player is outside, THEN the war image still wins over the outside art
- GIVEN an assembly, THEN the tavern image is chosen
- GIVEN an assembly but the player is outside, THEN the tavern image still wins

### CourierDispatchPolicyTests.cs

_Courier audit (island report): ONE courier in flight per sender. Without this gate, every queued letter dispatched its own courier, and a slow or stuck courier (a sender on an island, a player at sea) snowballed into a whole pack of couriers riding from the same NPC. These tests pin the dedup rule: any undelivered, unlost letter still bearing a courier party id means the road is occupied, and no second courier may spawn for that sender until it clears._

- GIVEN no letters at all, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN an empty letter list, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN an undelivered letter with a courier, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is true
- GIVEN a delivered letter with a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a lost letter with a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a queued letter without a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN one in flight letter among history, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is true

### CourierRationPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the courier ration top-up boundary (courier robustness batch, ROADMAP 2026-07-20). The stake: a starved courier party is destroyed by the engine and its letter reported "lost on the road" with no visible cause; the top-up guarantees the pathological waits (the M5 landfall vigil) can never starve a courier._

- GIVEN a courier with any food left, WHEN asked, THEN no top up is granted
- GIVEN a courier whose larder is empty, WHEN asked, THEN the top up is granted

### CourierStuckPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a live courier's stillness becomes a jam (courier robustness batch, ROADMAP 2026-07-20). The stake: a jammed courier used to burn the whole delivery grace window and then LOSE the letter; these two predicates are what hands the letter back to date-based delivery early enough for it to still arrive._

- GIVEN drift within or at the epsilon, WHEN asked, THEN it does not count as movement
- GIVEN progress beyond the epsilon, WHEN asked, THEN it counts as movement
- GIVEN one day of stillness, WHEN asked, THEN the courier is not yet stuck
- GIVEN stillness reaching the threshold, WHEN asked, THEN the courier is stuck

### CourierWaylayPolicyTests.cs

_Player report (2026-07-09): every courier sent was waylaid, every single time, losing the typed letter for good. Design ruling (2026-07-10): a courier is now IGNORED by default; each bandit party it passes rolls independently (about 1 in 3) to turn hostile and give chase, and even then a fast courier usually outruns the chase. These two independent rolls are what keeps real interception rare and earned, instead of the routine loss the report described._

- GIVEN a roll, WHEN deciding hostility, THEN only the bottom third turns hostile
- GIVEN a negative or out of range roll, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not hostile
- GIVEN the full roll range, WHEN counting hostiles, THEN it is about one in three
- GIVEN a chase, WHEN deciding the catch, THEN the fast courier usually escapes
- GIVEN both filters, WHEN combined, THEN the effective per band waylay is well under the chase rate
- GIVEN no band has rolled yet, WHEN checking the budget, THEN a band may still roll
- GIVEN one band has rolled, WHEN checking the budget, THEN a second band may still roll
- GIVEN the cap of bands has rolled, WHEN checking the budget, THEN no further band may roll

### CourtActionPolicyTests.cs

_This is the "hollow promise" fix (CHANGELOG v1.20.5): before this pillar a lord could agree in chat to lend troops or influence and nothing would actually happen. CourtActionPolicy is the pure math the bridge re-validates at execution time ("the prompt is advice, the bridge is law") for every court verb (give_influence, lend_troops, sway_opinion, pledge_against, release_prisoner, plus the give_troops/ spend_influence reciprocals). A wrong number here either breaks a promise the LLM just made to the player, lets an NPC give away resources (their own troops, their clan's influence) no real lord would part with, or lets a verb reach a target it must never touch (an NPC's own close kin, or a faction leader whose fate is reserved for the leader-only war-decision system, never a conversation)._

- GIVEN zero or negative relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a modest relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN a very deep relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a clan with far more influence than the grant, WHEN checking affordability, THEN it can afford it
- GIVEN a clan with barely more influence than the grant, WHEN checking affordability, THEN it cannot afford it
- GIVEN exactly the buffer multiple, WHEN checking affordability, THEN the threshold is inclusive
- GIVEN zero or negative relation, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a generous relation and ample room, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN an extremely deep relation, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a small party, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN the npc never drops below 60 percent strength
- GIVEN the player party nearly full, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it never exceeds the players room
- GIVEN no spare troops, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a party below the weakened fraction of a large enough size limit, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN it is
- GIVEN a party at or above the weakened fraction, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN it is not
- GIVEN a size limit below the minimum worth reinforcing, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN a tiny escort never counts
- GIVEN relation below the charity floor, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN ample spare troops and room, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN an extremely deep relation, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it is capped
